## 4:43/q22 When is water legally unavailable?  [فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q22/p1 Water is physically absent — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الحسن وعطاء): Al-Hasan and ʿAtaʾ say the explicit condition of finding no water bars everyone from tayammum when water is available; Abu ʿUmar says this would be sound without the majority view and transmitted reports. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (بعض من ألف في أحكام القرآن): A writer on Quranic rulings argues that because “water” is indefinite, tayammum is forbidden whenever any amount of water exists, mixed with other matter or alone. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi argues that “not finding water” means one must first seek it, and tayammum is a necessity purification like that of a woman with continuous bleeding. BAYDAWI-FULL, d. 685 (author): “Finding no water” includes being unable to use it, since what one is barred from using counts as unavailable. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (عمران بن حصين): In a report in both Sahih collections, the Prophet asks a Muslim who sat apart from the prayer group why he did not pray; the man says he is junub and has no water, and the Prophet tells him that clean earth suffices. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم): Ammar reports that the Prophet camped at Awlat al-Jaysh with Aisha; after her Dhafar-bead necklace was lost and water was unavailable until dawn, God granted tayammum. The Muslims struck earth without taking up dust and wiped faces and hands to shoulders and armpits. BIQAI-FULL, d. 885 (author): Not finding water includes either its absence or inability to use it. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (حذيفة): Hudhayfa reports the Prophet saying that earth was made a purifying substance for the community when water is unavailable. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): Naming the events before waterlessness signals that lack of water licenses relief only after purification becomes obligatory. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (النبي): People in sandy country with no water for a month or two, including people in janaba, menstruation, and postpartum impurity, are told by the Prophet to use their land. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p318/r6) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p318/r7) supports this position. JISHUMI (source note JISHUMI:v2p1567/r19) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p546#2/r6) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p547/r5) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p547/r6) supports this position. [16 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الحسن وعطاء,بعض من ألف في أحكام القرآن); BAYDAWI-FULL; IBNKATHIR-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم,عمران بن حصين); BIQAI-FULL; DURR-FULL(حذيفة); ALUSI-FULL(النبي); THALABI; JISHUMI; KASHSHAF-FULL; QURTUBI-FULL+]
- 4:43/q22/p2 Unusable or inaccessible water counts as absent — NASAFI-FULL, d. 710 (author): Not finding water includes being unable to use it because it is absent, distant, inaccessible, or blocked by a snake, predator, or enemy. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): Being blocked from water by an enemy, predator, or other obstacle is treated like lacking water. BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Not finding water means being unable to use it because it is absent or distant, access tools are lacking, or a snake, beast, or enemy blocks it; tayammum means seeking clean earth. [3 notes: NASAFI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL; BURSEVI]

## 4:43/q23 Must one search for water before tayammum?  [فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ · law]
- 4:43/q23/p1 A search is required — RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (الشافعي): Al-Shāfiʿī requires a fresh search for water when each prayer time begins after a person has prayed using tayammum, since not finding it implies prior searching. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): Malik's apparent position, shared by al-Shafi'i, makes searching for water a condition for valid tayammum. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة): Abu Hanifa and Qadi Abu Muhammad ibn Nasr hold that searching for water is not a condition of valid tayammum. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi prefers requiring a search: the verse's “not finding water” presupposes looking, and a substitute is usable only when inability to obtain the original is certain. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة): Abu Hanifa, his two companions, and al-Muzani allow tayammum before the prayer time because searching for water is not a condition, analogizing to voluntary prayer. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو الفرج): Abu al-Faraj permits making up multiple missed prayers with one tayammum; this assumes water-search is not a condition, which Qurtubi rejects. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): Malik requires a separate tayammum for each obligatory prayer because the worshipper must seek water anew for each one. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi prefers a new tayammum for each prayer: God requires seeking water for each prayer, and tayammum is an incomplete necessity-purity that consensus says ends when water appears. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مطرف): A transmission from Ibn al-Qasim through Abu Zayd, and reports from Mutarrif and Ibn al-Majishun, require repeating the second prayer forever because seeking water is a condition. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (كثير من الفقهاء): Many jurists infer that a person without water must first search for it; tayammum becomes permissible after searching and not finding any. IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (عائشة): Aisha reports in a sound collection that the Prophet stopped the party at al-Bayda or Dhat al-Jaysh to search for her lost necklace; they had no water, the verse of tayammum came down, and the necklace was found beneath her camel. IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Tayammum was legislated in year six during the expedition of al-Muraysiʿ, after the group found itself without water while searching for Aisha's lost necklace. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p318/r3) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p318/r4) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p318/r5) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p229/r4) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p229/r5) supports this position. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p58#2/r1) supports this position. Ibn ʿAṭiyya reports Isḥāq’s search only ahead and nearby, another group’s two bowshots, the Mālikī three-mile view, and al-Shāfiʿī’s limit against losing companions or prayer time; Ibn ʿAṭiyya praises the last view. [22 notes: RAZI-FULL(الشافعي); QURTUBI-FULL(أبو الفرج,أبو حنيفة,مالك,مطرف); IBNKATHIR-FULL(كثير من الفقهاء); IBNASHUR-FULL(عائشة); THALABI(أبو حنيفة); BAGHAWI-FULL(أبو حنيفة); IBNATIYYA-FULL(إسحق بن راهويه,فرقة,مالك); IBNATIYYA-FULL(الشافعي)+; QURTUBI-FULL+]

## 4:43/q24 When must one buy water?  [فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q24/p1 when obtaining water requires purchase, the duty depends on the buyer’s means and need; he should buy it unless its high price causes harm — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (author): When obtaining water requires purchase, the duty depends on the buyer’s means and need; he should buy it unless its high price causes harm. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p58#2/r14) supports this position. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p58#2/r15) supports this position. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p58#2/r16) supports this position. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p58#2/r17) supports this position. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p58#2/r18) supports this position. [6 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(أشهب,الحسن,طائفة); IBNATIYYA-FULL+]
- 4:43/q24/p2 one group allows paying for water only up to a one-third increase over its ordinary price; another allows paying two or three times its value, positions qurtubi says occur in maliki law — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (طائفة): One group allows paying for water only up to a one-third increase over its ordinary price; another allows paying two or three times its value, positions Qurtubi says occur in Maliki law. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(طائفة)]
- 4:43/q24/p3 ashhab says people should not be required to buy a water-skin for ten dirhams — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أشهب): Ashhab says people should not be required to buy a water-skin for ten dirhams. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أشهب)]
- 4:43/q24/p4 al-shafi'i rejects paying an increased price for water — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i rejects paying an increased price for water. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الشافعي)]
- 4:43/q24/p5 al-hasan says a person should buy water even by spending all his wealth and becoming destitute; qurtubi calls this weak because religion is ease — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الحسن): Al-Hasan says a person should buy water even by spending all his wealth and becoming destitute; Qurtubi calls this weak because religion is ease. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الحسن)]
- 4:43/q24/p6 there is no disagreement that a person who finds water at its ordinary price must buy it — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الفقهاء): There is no disagreement that a person who finds water at its ordinary price must buy it. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الفقهاء)]
- 4:43/q24/p7 for water priced above its ordinary value, abū ḥanīfa and al-shāfiʿī allow tayammum, whereas mālik requires buying it with all one's wealth — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (أبو حنيفة والشافعي): For water priced above its ordinary value, Abū Ḥanīfa and al-Shāfiʿī allow tayammum, whereas Mālik requires buying it with all one's wealth. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(أبو حنيفة والشافعي)]

## 4:43/q25 May a healthy resident without water use tayammum?  [فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q25/p1 A healthy resident may not rely on tayammum — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك وابن نافع): For a healthy resident restrained from accessing water, they say he neither prays nor makes up the prayer. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (أصبغ): Asbagh says the restrained resident does not pray but makes it up if using water would make him miss the prayer time. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafiʿi and al-Tabari bar tayammum for a healthy resident except when death is feared; they, al-Layth, and al-Tabari require prayer repetition when resident water is absent and time is feared. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الشافعي والطبري): Al-Shāfiʿī and al-Ṭabarī do not permit tayammum to a resident who lacks water. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p313/r8) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p313/r9) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p313/r10) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p313/r11) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p222/r4) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p222/r5) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p222/r6) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p222/r7) supports this position. [12 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(أصبغ,مالك وابن نافع); QURTUBI-FULL(الشافعي); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الشافعي والطبري); THALABI(أبو حنيفة,الشافعي ومحمد بن الحسن,مالك والأوزاعي وأبو يوسف); BAGHAWI-FULL(أبو حنيفة,الشافعي,النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم,مالك والأوزاعي)]
- 4:43/q25/p2 Pray by tayammum without repetition — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): One interpretation takes aw as wa, making the cause tayammum the minor event itself and supporting its use by residents. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (مالك وأبو حنيفة ومحمد): Mālik, Abū Ḥanīfa, and Muḥammad permit a resident without water to use tayammum. KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (Şâfiî ve bazı müctehidler): Some jurists, including Shafi'i, hold that a resident with water may not perform tayammum unless using water threatens life. [3 notes: QURTUBI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(مالك وأبو حنيفة ومحمد); KURANYOLU-TEFSIR(Şâfiî ve bazı müctehidler)]
- 4:43/q25/p3 Pray by tayammum and repeat — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (ابن القاسم): For a healthy resident restrained from water, Ibn al-Qasim says to pray and repeat the prayer. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (أشهب): For a healthy resident restrained from water, Ashhab says to pray without repeating. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): For a resident who tayammums, the famous and correct Maliki view does not require repeating once water is found; Ibn Habib and Muhammad b. ʿAbd al-Hakam require repetition. [3 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(أشهب,ابن القاسم); QURTUBI-FULL(مالك)+]

## 4:43/q26 What if some, but insufficient, water is available?  [فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ · law]
- 4:43/q26/p1 Insufficient water is treated as unavailable — RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (الشافعي): Al-Shāfiʿī requires using any available amount insufficient for full ablution together with tayammum, relying on the verse’s apparent wording. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): Malik, Abu Hanifa, al-Shafi'i in one opinion, and most scholars require enough water for the whole purification; less than the needed amount is treated as unavailable and is not used. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319/r3) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319/r4) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319/r5) supports this position. [5 notes: RAZI-FULL(الشافعي); THALABI(الشافعي,الشافعي وأبو حنيفة ومالك والمزني); QURTUBI-FULL(مالك)+]

turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.

- 4:43/q27/p2 a second account says ʿāʾisha borrowed a necklace from asmāʾ and lost it; the prophet sent men to search, and when prayer came without water they prayed without ablution and reported it, whereupon the tayammum — IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (عائشة): A second account says ʿĀʾisha borrowed a necklace from Asmāʾ and lost it; the Prophet sent men to search, and when prayer came without water they prayed without ablution and reported it, whereupon the tayammum verse was revealed. ## 4:43/q28 What does tayammamū mean?  [فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ · meaning]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q28/p1 tayammum in arabic means to intend or head toward something; ibn ʿatiyya supports the sense with verses by imruʾ al-qays and al-aʿshā — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (author): Tayammum in Arabic means to intend or head toward something; Ibn ʿAtiyya supports the sense with verses by Imruʾ al-Qays and al-Aʿshā. - 4:43/q28/p2 tayammamu means deliberately aim, seek, and intend — TABRISI, d. 548 (author): Tayammamu means deliberately aim, seek, and intend. [1 notes: TABRISI]
- 4:43/q28/p3 tayammum means intending; in law it is the specific act of aiming at the earth and applying soil to designated limbs — TABRISI, d. 548 (author): Tayammum means intending; in law it is the specific act of aiming at the earth and applying soil to designated limbs. [1 notes: TABRISI]
- 4:43/q28/p4 tayammum means to intend or aim; the author says he discussed this earlier under “do not aim at the bad.” — IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (author): Tayammum means to intend or aim; the author says he discussed this earlier under “do not aim at the bad.” | mentions 2:267 [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD]
- 4:43/q28/p5 tayammum means intending or aiming; the forms ammamtuhu, tayammamtuhu, and taʾammamtuhu all mean i intended it — RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (author): Tayammum means intending or aiming; the forms ammamtuhu, tayammamtuhu, and taʾammamtuhu all mean I intended it. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 4:43/q28/p6 abū ḥanīfa permits tayammum by striking and wiping on bare rock without dust, since ṣaʿīd means what rises from the earth and the command is to intend land — RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (أبو حنيفة): Abū Ḥanīfa permits tayammum by striking and wiping on bare rock without dust, since ṣaʿīd means what rises from the earth and the command is to intend land. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(أبو حنيفة)]
- 4:43/q28/p7 in arabic, tayammum means intending or aiming: one aims at a thing or deliberately turns toward soil; usage later narrowed toward ritual wiping — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): In Arabic, tayammum means intending or aiming: one aims at a thing or deliberately turns toward soil; usage later narrowed toward ritual wiping. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q28/p8 al-khalil rejects replacing tayammam with ammam in the cited verse because shazran means obliquely, from the side, rather than straight ahead — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الخليل): Al-Khalil rejects replacing tayammam with ammam in the cited verse because shazran means obliquely, from the side, rather than straight ahead. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الخليل)]
- 4:43/q28/p9 al-shafi'i's verse says that wherever he aims, he carries his knowledge with him, another witness for tayammum as intention — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i's verse says that wherever he aims, he carries his knowledge with him, another witness for tayammum as intention. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الشافعي)]
- 4:43/q28/p10 tayammum means deliberately turning to clean earth; the ḥanafīs allow hard stone, while al-bayḍāwī’s school requires dust to adhere, reading “from it” partitively — BAYDAWI-FULL, d. 685 (author): Tayammum means deliberately turning to clean earth; the Ḥanafīs allow hard stone, while al-Bayḍāwī’s school requires dust to adhere, reading “from it” partitively. [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 4:43/q28/p11 tayammum linguistically means intending or aiming for something; the verse of imru al-qays uses the verb for heading toward a spring — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (author): Tayammum linguistically means intending or aiming for something; the verse of Imru al-Qays uses the verb for heading toward a spring. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL]
- 4:43/q28/p12 tayammum means sincerely intending the act and entering into it with that intention; ṣaʿīd is soil and ṭayyib is pure and wholesome — BIQAI-FULL, d. 885 (author): Tayammum means sincerely intending the act and entering into it with that intention; ṣaʿīd is soil and ṭayyib is pure and wholesome. [1 notes: BIQAI-FULL]
- 4:43/q28/p13 sufyan says tayammum means seeking or deliberately aiming for clean earth — DURR-FULL, d. 911 (سفيان): Sufyan says tayammum means seeking or deliberately aiming for clean earth. [1 notes: DURR-FULL(سفيان)]
- 4:43/q28/p14 the sense pure best fits purification: aim for a clean part of the earth's surface — ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): The sense pure best fits purification: aim for a clean part of the earth's surface. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL+]
- 4:43/q28/p15 tayammum literally means intending; al-aʿsha's line illustrates the sense — ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): Tayammum literally means intending; al-Aʿsha's line illustrates the sense. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 4:43/q28/p16 tayammum literally means intending, so intention is intrinsic to it — ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): Tayammum literally means intending, so intention is intrinsic to it. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 4:43/q28/p17 tayammum means intending, and saʿid means the earth's surface; dhu al-rumma's verse about an animal on the saʿid supports that sense — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Tayammum means intending, and saʿid means the earth's surface; Dhu al-Rumma's verse about an animal on the saʿid supports that sense. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]

## 4:43/q29 What does ṣaʿīd mean?  [صَعِيدًۭا · meaning]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q29/p1 The earth's surface — TABRISI, d. 548 (ذو الرمة): Dhu al-Rumma’s simile uses saʿid for the earth’s surface, where a fly strikes with its proboscis at midday. TABRISI, d. 548 (الزجاج): Al-Zajjaj says saʿid is the earth’s surface, whether soil or another material, named for the farthest surface one rises to from the earth’s interior. TABRISI, d. 548 (الزجاج): Al-Zajjaj says linguists agree saʿid is the earth’s surface, supporting the view that tayammum is valid using stone even without soil. TABRISI, d. 548 (author): Saʿid is the earth’s surface without vegetation or trees. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi explains ṣaʿīd as the earth's surface and traces its name to being the upper limit to which one rises. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): If ṣaʿīd means the earth's surface, it is a place where tayammum is performed; if it means soil, it is the direct object with an omitted preposition, and ṭayyib is its adjective or, in the lawful reading, an adverbial or verbal noun. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الخليل): Al-Khalīl says ṣaʿīd is what rises from the earth's surface, meaning the face of the earth. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الزجاج): Al-Zajjāj defines ṣaʿīd as the earth's surface, whether soil or another material, including bare rock; another scholar adds sand, minerals, and saline earth. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (مالك): Malik interprets sa‘id as anything on the earth’s surface, including soil, sand, trees, stones, and plants. BURSEVI, d. 1127 (الزجاج): Al-Zajjaj defines ṣaʿid as the earth’s surface, whether soil or another material, including bare rock. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (الخليل وثعلب): Al-Khalil and Thaʿlab define ṣaʿīd as the earth's surface; al-Zajjaj says linguists do not disagree. ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): Sa'id means the earth's surface, including stone and soil, so tayammum on a stone is valid even without dust adhering to the hand. STUDYQURAN (al-Ṭabarī): Al-Ṭabarī says ṣaʿīd is any pure or clean part of the earth’s surface; dust adhering to the hands purifies. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p81/r5) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p81/r6) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p81/r7) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p81/r8) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p81/r9) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p81/r10) supports this position. ZAJJAJ (source note ZAJJAJ:v2p55/r6) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p546#2/r7) supports this position. [33 notes: TABRISI(الزجاج,ذو الرمة); QURTUBI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الخليل,الزجاج); IBNKATHIR-FULL(مالك); BURSEVI(الزجاج); ALUSI-FULL(الخليل وثعلب); ELMALILI; STUDYQURAN(al-Ṭabarī); TAB-FULL(آخرون,ابن زيد,عمرو بن قيس الملائي,قتادة); ZAJJAJ; IBNABIHATIM(ابن عباس,حماد); MAWARDI-FULL(ابن زيد,ذو الرمة,علي، ابن مسعود، الشافعي,قتادة); JISHUMI(أبو مسلم,ابن زيد); KASHSHAF-FULL(الزجاج)]
- 4:43/q29/p2 Soil or dust specifically — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (الشافعي وطائفة): Al-Shafiʿi and a group understand ṭayyib as growth-producing, like 7:58, so ṣaʿīd means soil and tayammum is not allowed on other materials. | mentions 7:58 IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (علي، ابن مسعود، الفراء، أبو عبيد، الزجاج، ابن قتيبة): These authorities say al-ṣaʿīd means soil. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (علي): Ali says ṣaʿīd means soil specifically. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (عمرو بن قيس الملائي): Amr ibn Qays al-Mulaʿi says al-saʿid means soil. [4 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(الشافعي وطائفة); IBNJAWZI-ZAD(علي، ابن مسعود، الفراء، أبو عبيد، الزجاج، ابن قتيبة); QURTUBI-FULL(علي); DURR-FULL(عمرو بن قيس الملائي)]
- 4:43/q29/p3 Level or bare ground — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi cites 18:8, describing barren ground, to illustrate ṣaʿīd as the earth's surface. | mentions 18:8 ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الليث وقتادة): Al-Layth and Qatāda define ṣaʿīd as level, smooth ground without trees or plants. [2 notes: QURTUBI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الليث وقتادة)]

## 4:43/q30 What does ṭayyib mean?  [طَيِّبًۭا · meaning]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q30/p1 Pure and clean — TABRISI, d. 548 (author): Tayyiban means pure. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): Malik, Abu Hanifa, al-Thawri, and al-Tabari allow tayammum on any part of the earth, including soil, sand, stones, minerals, or saline ground; here ṭayyib means pure. NASAFI-FULL, d. 710 (author): Tayyib means pure. ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): Tayyib means thoroughly clean, excluding contaminated or doubtful material. IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Tayyib means pure, extending the permitted surface material to soil, sand, and stone when free of filth. MUQATIL (source note MUQATIL:4:43/r10) supports this position. IBNQUTAYBA-GHARIB (source note IBNQUTAYBA-GHARIB:v1p127#3/r4) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p81/r11) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p82/r1) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p82/r2) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p82/r3) supports this position. IBNABIHATIM (source note IBNABIHATIM:v3p962/r3) supports this position. IBNABIHATIM (source note IBNABIHATIM:v3p962/r4) supports this position. MAWARDI-FULL (source note MAWARDI-FULL:v1p490/r13) supports this position. MAWARDI-FULL (source note MAWARDI-FULL:v1p490/r14) supports this position. MAWARDI-FULL (source note MAWARDI-FULL:v1p490/r15) supports this position. MAWARDI-FULL (source note MAWARDI-FULL:v1p490/r16) supports this position. WAHIDI-WAJIZ (source note WAHIDI-WAJIZ:4:43-44/r10) supports this position. [18 notes: TABRISI; QURTUBI-FULL(مالك); NASAFI-FULL; ELMALILI; IBNASHUR-FULL; MUQATIL; IBNQUTAYBA-GHARIB; TAB-FULL(سفيان,عطاء); IBNABIHATIM(سعيد بن بشير,سفيان); MAWARDI-FULL(أبو جعفر الطبري,ابن جريج,ابن عباس,سفيان); WAHIDI-WAJIZ]
- 4:43/q30/p2 Lawful — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (طائفة): One group reads ṭayyib as meaning lawful, a sense Ibn ʿAtiyya considers awkward in this context. TABRISI, d. 548 (سفيان): Sufyan explains ṭayyib as lawful. IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (author): Two interpretations of ṭayyib are pure and lawful. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (فرقة): Another group interprets ṭayyib as lawful, a reading Qurtubi calls strained. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (سفيان الثوري): Sufyān al-Thawrī and others say ṭayyib means lawful. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (author): The adjective tayyib is interpreted either as lawful or as free from impurity. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم): Jabir reports five privileges given to the Prophet: victory through fear over a month’s distance, earth as mosque and purifier, lawful spoils, intercession, and a message to all people rather than one people; the report is in both Sahih collections. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (سفيان): Sufyan says “clean” means lawful for you. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (سفيان): Sufyan glosses ṭayyib as lawful; another sense is plant-growing rather than saline, as in 7:58. | mentions 7:58 IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Wine was still originally lawful and some Muslims drank it; after 2:219 some argued they drank for its benefits rather than its sin, while the verse signaled likely prohibition and prepared them for it without yet removing the allowance. | mentions 2:219 [10 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(طائفة); TABRISI(سفيان); IBNJAWZI-ZAD; QURTUBI-FULL(فرقة); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(سفيان الثوري); IBNKATHIR-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); DURR-FULL(سفيان); ALUSI-FULL(سفيان); IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 4:43/q30/p3 Fertile or cultivated — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (author): The agreed valid case is tayammum on pure, fertile soil that has not been moved or usurped. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i and Abu Yusuf restrict ṣaʿīd to fertile, growing soil and read ṭayyib as good soil, citing the verse that good land yields plants; al-Shafi'i requires dusty soil. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas answers that cultivated earth is the best ṣaʿīd; Abu Umar says this implies ṣaʿīd can also refer to earth that is not cultivated. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The consensus case for tayammum is clean, fertile soil that has not been moved or usurped; consensus bars gold, silver, gems, food, and impure substances. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الشافعي وابن عباس): Al-Shāfiʿī and a group take ṭayyib as fertile; Ibn ʿAbbās cites the good land that produces vegetation, yielding soil as ṣaʿīd, while this group permits other materials too. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): The fertile reading cites the verse والبلد الطيب يخرج نباته. | mentions 7:58 ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): All agree clean, fertile, unmoved, unappropriated soil is valid for tayammum and forbid gold, silver, gems, food, and impure substances. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (ابن عباس): A report raised to the Prophet says, in answer to a question, that cultivated earth is the purest soil. [8 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL; QURTUBI-FULL(ابن عباس,الشافعي); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الشافعي وابن عباس); DURR-FULL(ابن عباس)]

## 4:43/q31 Which materials can be used for tayammum?  [صَعِيدًۭا طَيِّبًۭا · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q31/p1 Materials on or attached to earth may serve — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك): Malik’s Mudawwana prescribes two strikes for tayammum; Ibn al-Jahm says one, and Malik elsewhere says one suffices. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (جمهور المذهب): Most Maliki jurists forbid tayammum on wood, while the Mukhtaṣar of al-Waqqar allows it; the school also differs about plants. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك): The Mudawwana allows tayammum with snow, while Malik prohibits it in other works. TABRISI, d. 548 (سعيد بن المسيب): A report from Saʿid ibn al-Musayyib says one strike suffices when tayammum replaces wudu: wipe the face from hairline to nose tip and hands from wrists to fingertips. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The report shows fear, not certainty, suffices; it calls a person who tayammums junub and supports his leading worshippers with ablution, which al-Qurtubi prefers among two Maliki views. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الجمهور): Most jurists prohibit tayammum on wood, though Mukhtasar al-Waqqar allows it; one view distinguishes attached from detached wood. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الأوزاعي): Al-Awza'i and al-Thawri allow tayammum on earth and everything on it, including trees, stones, rocks, ice, and snow; al-Tha'labi attributes to Malik permission to strike a tree and wipe with the hand. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (جابر بن عبدالله وابن عمر): Jabir and Ibn Umar transmit from the Prophet that tayammum reaches the elbows; Ibn Umar held this practice. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (مالك والشافعي): Mālik permits minerals for tayammum, while al-Shāfiʿī prohibits them; salt, snow, moved soil, brick, walls, plants, sticks, and trees are disputed. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (author): Tayammum substitutes for wudu as purification, not for every body part: face and hands alone suffice by consensus, though jurists disagree about the method. BURSEVI, d. 1127 (أبو حنيفة): According to Abu Hanifa, striking a bare rock and wiping with it suffices for dry ablution even when it has no dust. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (مالك): Malik permits tayammum with snow; al-Alusi notes Shiite criticism and says he defended Malik in a separate work. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): The events prepare for the water condition and show that it licenses relief after the cause of purification arises for either minor or major impurity. ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): God's pardon explains the tayammum concession; it does not license intoxication or neglecting purification. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r1) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r2) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r3) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r4) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319#2/r5) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319#2/r6) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319#2/r7) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319#2/r8) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319#2/r9) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p226/r4) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p226/r5) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p226/r6) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p226/r7) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p226/r8) supports this position. [28 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(جمهور المذهب,مالك); TABRISI(سعيد بن المسيب); QURTUBI-FULL(الأوزاعي,الجمهور,جابر بن عبدالله وابن عمر); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(مالك والشافعي); IBNKATHIR-FULL; BURSEVI(أبو حنيفة); ALUSI-FULL(مالك); ELMALILI; TAB-FULL; THALABI(أبو جهيم,أبو حنيفة,الأوزاعي والثوري,الشافعي,مالك); BAGHAWI-FULL(أصحاب الرأي,الشافعي,بعضهم); QURTUBI-FULL+]
- 4:43/q31/p2 Material ruling varies — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (أهل المذهب): The Maliki school allows tayammum on both mineral salt and solid salt; other views forbid both or allow mineral salt but forbid solid salt. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (جمهور المذهب): Most Maliki jurists permit tayammum with soil carried in a dish, though some within the school and many outside it prohibit it. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (أهل المذهب): The school has two positions on tayammum with baked materials such as brick or plaster: permitted or forbidden. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (author): Jurists disagree over whether tayammum may be performed on a wall. RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (الشافعي): Al-Shāfiʿī requires soil that sticks to the hand for tayammum. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن الأنباري): Ibn al-Anbari explains saying a man has tayammama as having wiped soil on his face and hands; Qurtubi identifies this as ritual tayammum when done as worship. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن عطية): Ibn Atiyya says most Malikis allow tayammum on moved soil, though a Maliki minority forbids it; baked lime and brick have two views, and the wall is disputed. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi says the Bukhari-transmitted report of the Prophet wiping on a wall proves tayammum is valid without soil, supporting Malik over al-Shafi'i. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i analogizes tayammum to wiping the head: just as water must be carried to the head, soil must be carried to the limbs. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi says the wall report proves tayammum is valid without soil, against al-Shafi'i's requirement for pure, adherent dust. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الثوري): Al-Thawri and Ahmad permit tayammum with dust from felt. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الثوري وأحمد): Al-Thawrī and Aḥmad permit tayammum with dust on the hand. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (أبو جهيم): Abu Juhaym says he saw the Prophet urinate and leave his greeting unanswered, then perform tayammum on a wall, wiping his face and hands to the elbows before returning the greeting. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (أبو حنيفة ومحمد): Abu Hanifa and one report from Muhammad permit tayammum with kohl, brick, litharge, gems, or coral without dust. STUDYQURAN (a ḥadīth): A hadith lists God’s making dust purifying for Muslims among His blessings favoring them over other peoples. [15 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(أهل المذهب,جمهور المذهب); RAZI-FULL(الشافعي); QURTUBI-FULL(ابن الأنباري,ابن عطية,الثوري,الشافعي); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الثوري وأحمد); IBNKATHIR-FULL(أبو جهيم); ALUSI-FULL(أبو حنيفة ومحمد); STUDYQURAN(a ḥadīth); QURTUBI-FULL+]
- 4:43/q31/p3 Earth surfaces, including stone, may serve — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك): Malik permits tayammum on any part of the earth—soil, sand, stones, minerals, or saline ground—understanding ṭayyib as pure. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (author): Jurists agree tayammum is forbidden on pure gold, silver, precious stones, food, and impure substances. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك): Malik allows tayammum on minerals, while al-Shafiʿi forbids it; al-Lakhmi notes disagreement within the Maliki school as well. RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (author): Rāzī favors soil as the cautious meaning of good ṣaʿīd: non-saline earth, whose use is agreed valid, is specified in Prophetic reports as purifier. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم): The Prophet tells Abu Dharr that clean earth is a Muslim's purification even without water for ten years; the opposing jurists treat this as equating soil purification with water ablution. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الخليل): Al-Khalil's lexicon, as cited by Ibn Faris, glosses tayammum with ṣaʿīd as taking its dust, implying soil because smooth stone has no dust. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): Malik and others allow tayammum on minerals, while al-Shafi'i and others prohibit it. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة): Al-Tha'labi says Abu Hanifa permits tayammum with kohl, arsenic, lime, gypsum, and powdered gems, but not metal filings because they are not of the earth's genus. NASAFI-FULL, d. 710 (author): Dry ablution is valid on bare rock even without dust if the person strikes it and wipes with it. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (author): Ibn Kathir recalls Hudhayfa’s report in Muslim that this community’s rows resemble angels’ and that earth is a mosque and its soil purification when water is absent. STUDYQURAN (author): Precious metals and gems are unsuitable as clean earth for tayammum. STUDYQURAN (author): Tayammum requires intention, then two contacts with earth or stone: one to wipe the face, one to wipe the hands. [12 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(مالك); QURTUBI-FULL(أبو حنيفة,الخليل,رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم,مالك); NASAFI-FULL; IBNKATHIR-FULL; STUDYQURAN; RAZI-FULL+]
- 4:43/q31/p4 Musk or saffron may serve — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (ابن علية وابن كيسان): Al-Naqqash reports that Ibn ʿUlayya and Ibn Kaysan allowed tayammum with musk and saffron; Ibn ʿAtiyya rejects this as wholly erroneous. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (author): Ibn ʿAtiyya calls the permission to use musk and saffron for tayammum wholly erroneous. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن عطية): Al-Naqqash reports that Ibn Ulayya and Ibn Kaysan permit tayammum with musk or saffron; Ibn Atiyya calls this completely mistaken. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (ابن علية وابن كيسان): Ibn ʿUlayya and Ibn Kaysān permit tayammum using musk and saffron. [4 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(ابن علية وابن كيسان); QURTUBI-FULL(ابن عطية); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن علية وابن كيسان)]
- 4:43/q31/p5 Dust-bearing soil is required — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (حذيفة بن اليمان): Muslim transmits Hudhayfa’s report that earth is a mosque and its soil is purification when water is absent; the soil-only jurists argue that this wording specifies soil in a statement of divine favor. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (الشافعي): From Quran 5:6’s ‘from it,’ al-Shafi‘i infers that tayammum must use pure dusty soil whose dust adheres to the face and hands. | mentions 5:6 [2 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(الشافعي,حذيفة بن اليمان)]

## 4:43/q32 What intention and prayer time does tayammum require?  [فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q32/p1 some maliki jurists require repeating tayammum within the prayer time after using one strike, while ibn nafiʿ requires repeating it ever after — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (غيره في المذهب): Some Maliki jurists require repeating tayammum within the prayer time after using one strike, while Ibn Nafiʿ requires repeating it ever after. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p317/r2) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p317/r3) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p317/r4) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p317/r5) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319#2/r3) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p319#2/r4) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p226/r9) supports this position. [8 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(غيره في المذهب); THALABI(علي,علي وابن عباس وابن حمزة ومالك والشافعي والليث بن سعد وأحمد بن حنبل,عمرو بن قيس); BAGHAWI-FULL]
- 4:43/q32/p2 abū ḥanīfa holds that a person who already searched and prayed with tayammum need not search again when the next prayer time begins — RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (أبو حنيفة): Abū Ḥanīfa holds that a person who already searched and prayed with tayammum need not search again when the next prayer time begins. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(أبو حنيفة)]
- 4:43/q32/p3 al-awzāʿī and al-ḥasan say ablution and tayammum are valid without intention — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الأوزاعي والحسن): Al-Awzāʿī and al-Ḥasan say ablution and tayammum are valid without intention. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الأوزاعي والحسن)]
- 4:43/q32/p4 abū ḥanīfa and his companions require intention for tayammum but not water purification, by analogy with removing physical impurity without intention; al-walīd transmits this from mālik — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة وأصحابه): Abū Ḥanīfa and his companions require intention for tayammum but not water purification, by analogy with removing physical impurity without intention; al-Walīd transmits this from Mālik. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو حنيفة وأصحابه)]
- 4:43/q32/p5 when water seems impossible to find during the prayer time, tayammum and prayer early are recommended so the worshipper secures the virtue of praying early — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): When water seems impossible to find during the prayer time, tayammum and prayer early are recommended so the worshipper secures the virtue of praying early. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q32/p6 tayammum is obligatory for every accountable person owing prayer when water is absent and prayer time has entered — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Tayammum is obligatory for every accountable person owing prayer when water is absent and prayer time has entered. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q32/p7 al-shafi'i and the first group disallow tayammum before prayer time because the verse links it to a need that does not exist before the time — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i and the first group disallow tayammum before prayer time because the verse links it to a need that does not exist before the time. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الشافعي)]
- 4:43/q32/p8 they require tayammum to reach the elbows by analogy with ablution; ibn nafi requires repeating forever if one stops at the wrists, while malik requires repetition within prayer time — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة والشافعي وأصحابهما والثوري وابن أبي سلمة والليث): They require tayammum to reach the elbows by analogy with ablution; Ibn Nafi requires repeating forever if one stops at the wrists, while Malik requires repetition within prayer time. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو حنيفة والشافعي وأصحابهما والثوري وابن أبي سلمة والليث)]
- 4:43/q32/p9 abū yūsuf and zufar allow tayammum only when there is fear of missing prayer time — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (أبو يوسف وزفر): Abū Yūsuf and Zufar allow tayammum only when there is fear of missing prayer time. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(أبو يوسف وزفر)]

## 4:43/q33 How many strikes does tayammum require?  [فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q33/p1 Two strikes — TABRISI, d. 548 (عمار بن ياسر ومكحول والطبري): Ammar and Makhul hold for two strikes, one for the face and one for the hands to the wrists; al-Tabari prefers this view. IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (عمار بن ياسر، الزهري): A third view extends wiping from the fingertips to the armpits, citing ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir's report of a travel concession and two strikes, one for the face and one to the shoulders and armpits; al-Zuhrī holds this view. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك والأوزاعي والشافعي وأبو حنيفة والثوري والليث): They require two strikes, one for the face and another for the hands; this method is transmitted from the Prophet by Jabir and Ibn Umar. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن أبي ليلى والحسن بن حي): They require two strikes, each wiping the face, forearms, and elbows. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو عمر): Abu ʿUmar resolves conflicting reports by the Qur’an’s apparent wording: two strikes, face then hands to the elbows, by analogy with ablution and following Ibn Umar; a sound Prophetic report would govern. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (مالك وأبو حنيفة والشافعي والثوري والليث وابن أبي سلمة): Mālik in al-Mudawwana, Abū Ḥanīfa, al-Shāfiʿī, al-Thawrī, al-Layth, and Ibn Abī Salama require two strikes, one for face and one for hands. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (ابن أبي ليلى والحسن): Ibn Abī Laylā and al-Ḥasan also require two strikes, each wiping face, arms, and elbows; Abū Ḥayyān says no other scholar held this. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi‘i’s later position requires two strikes, wiping the face and hands up to the elbows; he argues that ‘hands’ has several ranges and should be read in light of the ablution verse because both concern purification. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (ابن عمر): Al-Daraqutni transmits a marfu‘ report from Ibn Umar prescribing two strikes, one for the face and one for the hands to the elbows; Ibn Kathir rejects its reliability because weak narrators occur in its chains. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi‘i’s earlier position requires two strikes and wiping the face and hands to the wrists or palms. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (ابن عمر): A report raised to the Prophet says tayammum uses two strikes: one for the face and one for the arms up to the elbows. ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): The practical method is to strike the earth once and wipe the face, then strike again and wipe both hands up to the elbows. FARRA (source note FARRA:v1p268/r14) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p86/r1) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p86/r2) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p86/r3) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p86/r4) supports this position. ZAJJAJ (source note ZAJJAJ:v2p55/r5) supports this position. IBNABIHATIM (source note IBNABIHATIM:v3p962/r5) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p320/r3) supports this position. JISHUMI (source note JISHUMI:v2p1567/r24) supports this position. JISHUMI (source note JISHUMI:v2p1567/r25) supports this position. JISHUMI (source note JISHUMI:v2p1567/r26) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p227/r6) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p227/r7) supports this position. [25 notes: TABRISI(عمار بن ياسر ومكحول والطبري); IBNJAWZI-ZAD(عمار بن ياسر، الزهري); QURTUBI-FULL(أبو عمر,ابن أبي ليلى والحسن بن حي,مالك والأوزاعي والشافعي وأبو حنيفة والثوري والليث); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن أبي ليلى والحسن,مالك وأبو حنيفة والشافعي والثوري والليث وابن أبي سلمة); IBNKATHIR-FULL(ابن عمر,الشافعي); DURR-FULL(ابن عمر); ELMALILI; FARRA; TAB-FULL(ابن جابر,الشعبي,رسول الله,عمار بن ياسر); ZAJJAJ; IBNABIHATIM(قتادة); THALABI(ابن سيرين، عبد الله بن عمر، جابر بن عبد الله، الحسن البصري، الشعبي، أبو حنيفة، وحنبل، ومالك، والليث); JISHUMI(ابن سيرين,سعيد بن المسيب والأوزاعي وإسحاق وأحمد,علي وجابر وابن عمر والحسن والشعبي وأبو علي); BAGHAWI-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم,علي وابن عباس والشعبي وعطاء ومكحول والأوزاعي وأحمد وإسحاق)]
- 4:43/q33/p2 One strike — TABRISI, d. 548 (أكثر الفقهاء وأبو حنيفة والشافعي): Most jurists, Abu Hanifa, al-Shafiʿi, and some of al-Tabrisi’s school require one strike, wiping the hands to the elbows. IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (عمار عن النبي): One view limits the hands to the wrists, citing the Prophet's report that tayammum is one strike for the face and palms; this is held by Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib, ʿAṭāʾ ibn Abī Rabāḥ, ʿIkrima, al-Awzāʿī, Makḥūl, Mālik, Aḥmad, Isḥāq, and Dāwūd. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن أبي الجهم والأوزاعي وعطاء والشعبي وأحمد بن حنبل وإسحاق وداود والطبري): They hold that tayammum uses one strike; the author calls the report from ʿAmmar the strongest established evidence. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عمار): A Muslim report from ʿAmmār has the Prophet say one strike on the ground, blowing off dust, then wiping the face and palms suffices. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عمار): Another Muslim report describes one strike, wiping the left over the right, the backs of the hands, and the face. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عطاء والشعبي والأوزاعي وأحمد وإسحاق وداود والطبري): The apparent verse and sound reports indicate one strike for face and hands; this is held by ʿAṭāʾ, one report from al-Shaʿbī, al-Awzāʿī's best-known report, Aḥmad, Isḥāq, Dāwūd, and al-Ṭabarī. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (أحمد): Ahmad’s view is that a single strike suffices to wipe the face and palms, supported by reports from Ammar about the Prophet’s instruction. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (عمار): Ammar recalls that he and Umar became junub without water; he rolled in dust and prayed, and the Prophet taught that a single strike of earth, blowing on the hands, and wiping the face and palms sufficed. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (عمار): A separate report from Ammar explicitly states that tayammum is one strike for the face and palms. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (أبو موسى وابن مسعود): Abu Musa reminds Ibn Masud of Ammar’s report that the Prophet taught one strike for the face and hands; Ibn Masud replies that Umar was not persuaded, asks what to do with this verse, and says people might resort to tayammum merely because water feels cold. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (عائشة): Aisha reports that the Prophet demonstrated tayammum with one strike for the face and another for the hands. SUYUTI-LUBAB, d. 911 (الأسلع بن شريك): In al-Ṭabarānī's report, Jibrīl brought the dry-earth verse after al-Aslaʿ told the Prophet he was impure; the Prophet demonstrated one strike for the face and one for the hands to the elbows. [12 notes: TABRISI(أكثر الفقهاء وأبو حنيفة والشافعي); IBNJAWZI-ZAD(عمار عن النبي); QURTUBI-FULL(ابن أبي الجهم والأوزاعي وعطاء والشعبي وأحمد بن حنبل وإسحاق وداود والطبري); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(عطاء والشعبي والأوزاعي وأحمد وإسحاق وداود والطبري,عمار); IBNKATHIR-FULL(أبو موسى وابن مسعود,أحمد,عمار); DURR-FULL(عائشة); SUYUTI-LUBAB(الأسلع بن شريك)]
- 4:43/q33/p3 A described striking method — TABRISI, d. 548 (author): Al-Tabrisi’s school uses that two-strike method when tayammum replaces the major ablution after sexual impurity. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الجمهور): The majority follow the verse's order, wiping the face before the hands; a report in al-Bukhari's chapter on one-strike tayammum mentions the hands first, as do some scholars by analogy with reversing ablution order. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (ابن عمر): Ibn Umar reports the companions struck the earth twice, wiping the face after the first strike and the arms from elbows to wrists after the second. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (مكحول): Al-Tabari transmits Makḥul's view of a strike for the face and palms through the wrists. [4 notes: TABRISI; QURTUBI-FULL(الجمهور); DURR-FULL(ابن عمر); ALUSI-FULL(مكحول)]

## 4:43/q34 How much of the face must be wiped?  [بِوُجُوهِكُمْ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q34/p1 the part of the face wiped in tayammum is the same area delimited for washing in ablution — IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (author): The part of the face wiped in tayammum is the same area delimited for washing in ablution. MAWARDI-FULL (source note MAWARDI-FULL:v1p490/r17) supports this position. [2 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD; MAWARDI-FULL]
- 4:43/q34/p2 there is no dispute that the whole face must be covered in ablution and tayammum; some exempt folds and spaces between fingers, a position in the maliki school attributed to muhammad ibn maslama by ibn — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (محمد بن مسلمة): There is no dispute that the whole face must be covered in ablution and tayammum; some exempt folds and spaces between fingers, a position in the Maliki school attributed to Muhammad ibn Maslama by Ibn Atiyya. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(محمد بن مسلمة)]
- 4:43/q34/p3 when wiping is done with an instrument, the hand carries and moves that instrument across the surface being wiped — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): When wiping is done with an instrument, the hand carries and moves that instrument across the surface being wiped. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q34/p4 the apparent wording requires wiping the whole face, as ablution washes it all; some jurists allow leaving the creases untraced — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): The apparent wording requires wiping the whole face, as ablution washes it all; some jurists allow leaving the creases untraced. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 4:43/q34/p5 a report says the prophet struck a wall and wiped his face and arms; its transmitter muhammad ibn thabit was criticized, and reliable transmitters stop it at ibn umar. bukhari, abu zur‘a, and ibn ‘adi — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (ابن عمر والبخاري وأبو زرعة وابن عدي والبيهقي): A report says the Prophet struck a wall and wiped his face and arms; its transmitter Muhammad ibn Thabit was criticized, and reliable transmitters stop it at Ibn Umar. Bukhari, Abu Zur‘a, and Ibn ‘Adi call the stopped version correct; al-Bayhaqi calls raising it munkar. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(ابن عمر والبخاري وأبو زرعة وابن عدي والبيهقي)]
- 4:43/q34/p6 the bāʾ in “your faces” limits the wiping action because dust cannot reach every part; wiping is valid even if it does not cover the hairline — BIQAI-FULL, d. 885 (author): The bāʾ in “your faces” limits the wiping action because dust cannot reach every part; wiping is valid even if it does not cover the hairline. [1 notes: BIQAI-FULL]

## 4:43/q35 How far must the hands be wiped?  [وَأَيْدِيكُمْ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q35/p1 To the elbows — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك): Malik’s Mudawwana requires wiping to the elbows; stopping at the wrists requires repeating within the time, while Ibn Nafiʿ says to repeat ever after. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك): Malik’s Mudawwana describes wiping left fingertips over the right fingers and up to the elbow, then the inner forearm to the wrist, and repeating on the other arm. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (قوم): Others analogize tayammum to ablution and require wiping to the elbows; the majority also extend the wipe to the elbows. IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (ابن عباس عن النبي): A second view extends wiping to the elbows, citing Ibn ʿAbbās's report that the Prophet wiped his forearms; it is held by Ibn ʿUmar, his son Sālim, al-Ḥasan, Abū Ḥanīfa, and al-Shāfiʿī, while al-Shaʿbī is reported with both views. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم): A report through al-Sha‘bi, ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Abza, and ʿAmmar ibn Yasir has the Prophet specify wiping to the elbows. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (ابن عطية): Ibn ʿAṭiyya says he knew of no scholar who followed the report about wiping to mid-forearm. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (أبو حنيفة والشافعي والثوري والليث وابن أبي سلمة): Abū Ḥanīfa, al-Shāfiʿī, al-Thawrī, al-Layth, and others require wiping to the elbows; this is also attributed to Jābir, Ibn ʿUmar, al-Ḥasan, and Ibrāhīm. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (أبو مالك): Abu Malik reports that Ammar performed tayammum by wiping his face and hands without wiping his forearm. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (مكحول): Makhul limits tayammum to the face and hands through the wrists: unlike ablution, this verse does not specify elbows, and the thief's hand is cut at the wrist joint. | mentions 5:6, 5:38 BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): The hands are wiped to the elbows, based on a report that the Prophet did so and by analogy because tayammum replaces ablution and takes its measure. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (ابن عمر): Al-Hakim reports from Ibn ʿUmar that they wiped from elbows to palms over the hair-bearing areas on both sides. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (أبو داود): A report in Abu Dawud says the Prophet wiped his hands to the elbows; al-Alusi identifies this as his school's, al-Shafiʿi's, and most scholars' view. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): Wiping to the elbows is supported by analogy with ablution, the original purification; the majority also report its validity. MUQATIL (source note MUQATIL:4:43/r11) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r5) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p86/r5) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p88/r1) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p88/r2) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p88/r3) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p320/r1) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p320/r2) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p320/r4) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p320/r5) supports this position. MAWARDI-FULL (source note MAWARDI-FULL:v1p490/r18) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p227/r1) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p227/r2) supports this position. [45 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(قوم,مالك); IBNJAWZI-ZAD(ابن عباس عن النبي); QURTUBI-FULL(رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(أبو حنيفة والشافعي والثوري والليث وابن أبي سلمة,ابن عطية); DURR-FULL(أبو مالك,مكحول); BURSEVI; ALUSI-FULL(أبو داود,ابن عمر); MUQATIL; TAB-FULL(آخرون,أبو اليقظان,أبو جهيم,ابن عمر,الحسن,الشعبي,القائلون بالآباط,القائلون بالمرفقين,بعضهم,سالم بن عبد الله,عامر); THALABI(الزهري,النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم,رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم); MAWARDI-FULL(ابن عمر، الحسن، الشعبي، سالم بن عبد الله، الشافعي,الزهري، أبو بكر,عمار بن ياسر، مكحول، مالك، الشافعي); JISHUMI(ابن عمر والحسن والشعبي وأكثر الفقهاء,الزهري,عمار ومكحول); BAGHAWI-FULL(أبو الصمة,أكثر أهل العلم,الزهري)]
- 4:43/q35/p2 To shoulders or armpits — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (أبو بكر الصديق): Al-Tabari relates that Abu Bakr told ʿAʾisha the tayammum dispensation was a blessing, and that they struck once for the face and once for hands up to shoulders or armpits. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (الداودي): Al-Dawudi classifies the wrists as obligatory, elbows as sunnah, and armpits as meritorious; Ibn ʿAtiyya says this lacks analogy or evidence. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (قوم): Some generalize the word “hands” to require wiping from the shoulders. TABRISI, d. 548 (الزهري): Al-Zuhri reports a third view: wipe to the armpits. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن شهاب): Ibn Shihab says the hand wipe reaches the shoulders, a position also reported from Abu Bakr. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الزهري): Al-Zuhri argues from the general word ‘hands’ for wiping to the armpits; Makhul counters by asking where the hand is cut in the theft verse and says he defeated the argument. | mentions 5:38 QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الدراوردي): Al-Darawardi is reported to distinguish the wrists as obligatory and the armpits as meritorious. BAYDAWI-FULL, d. 685 (author): Although hand can mean the limb up to the shoulder, a Prophetic report of wiping to the elbows and analogy with ablution establish the limit here. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (ابن شهاب): Because hands can linguistically extend to the shoulders, Ibn Shihāb says wipe to the armpits; this is also reported from Abū Bakr. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (الزهري): Al-Zuhri says tayammum reaches the armpits. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (الزهري): Al-Tabari transmits al-Zuhri's view that tayammum wiping reaches the armpits. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p90/r3) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p227/r3) supports this position. [13 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(أبو بكر الصديق,الداودي,قوم); TABRISI(الزهري); QURTUBI-FULL(ابن شهاب,الدراوردي,الزهري); BAYDAWI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن شهاب); DURR-FULL(الزهري); ALUSI-FULL(الزهري); TAB-FULL(الزهري); BAGHAWI-FULL]
- 4:43/q35/p3 The hand wipe has a disputed limit — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو الجهيم بن الحارث بن الصمة الأنصاري): Abu al-Juhaym reports that the Prophet delayed answering a man's greeting until he wiped his face and hands on a wall; another route says he lacked ritual purity. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i reads “from it” in al-Ma'ida as requiring soil to be transferred to the limbs; Qurtubi and Malik do not require transfer because the Prophet struck the ground, lifted and blew or shook his hands, and also wiped on a wall. | mentions 5:6 ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): The apparent instruction is to wipe face and hands using pure earth; that manner suffices, and the conjunction does not order face before hands. BIQAI-FULL, d. 885 (author): The hands are wiped from the earth, as Sūrat al-Māʾida makes explicit; the wording can indicate that a stone suffices. | mentions 5:6 BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): The preposition bi is extra in the construction: wipe your faces and hands from the earth. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): The bāʾ is connective; the apparent ruling requires wiping the whole face and hands, so leaving any part unwiped invalidates tayammum. IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): The bāʾ is emphatic, as in 19:25 and al-Nabigha's verse; the meaning is simply wipe your faces and hands. | mentions 19:25 [7 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو الجهيم بن الحارث بن الصمة الأنصاري,الشافعي); ABUHAYYAN-FULL; BIQAI-FULL; BURSEVI; ALUSI-FULL; IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 4:43/q35/p4 To the wrists or palms — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مكحول وجماعة من العلماء): Some Maliki jurists require wiping only to the wrists; outside the school, some require only the palms, following ʿAmmar’s hadith and al-Shaʿbi’s position. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (ابن حبيب): Ibn Habib says the palms should then be wiped against each other; al-Lakhmi interprets the Mudawwana as requiring this, making Ibn Habib’s statement explanatory rather than contradictory. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (قوم): Some stop at the wrists, following the hadith; others analogize purification in tayammum to the legal hand amputation limit. IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (آخرون): Others follow ʿAmmar’s hadith and wipe only the palms. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (علي بن أبي طالب والأوزاعي وعطاء والشعبي وأحمد بن حنبل وإسحاق بن راهويه): Another group makes the wrists the obligatory limit; the view is also reported from Malik and was al-Shafi‘i’s earlier position. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الشعبي): Al-Shaʿbī says to wipe only the palms; some hadith jurists agree, and Abū Ḥayyān says this is the position to follow because its hadith is sound. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عمار): Another Muslim wording says the Prophet struck the earth, shook his hands, and wiped face and palms. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عمار): Al-Bukhārī's version says the Prophet brought his hands near his mouth, then wiped face and palms. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عمار): Abū Dāwūd's version says the Prophet struck the earth, grasped the hands, rubbed each palm against the other, then wiped the face. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (علي وعطاء والشعبي ومكحول والأوزاعي وأحمد وإسحاق وداود والطبري والشافعي): Another group wipes only to the wrists; this is reported from Mālik and was al-Shāfiʿī's earlier position. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (أبو هريرة): Abu Hurayra reports that when he sought guidance about tayammum, the Prophet urinated, struck the ground with his hands, and wiped his face and palms. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r6) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r7) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r8) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r9) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r10) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p83/r11) supports this position. [22 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(آخرون,ابن حبيب,قوم,مكحول وجماعة من العلماء); QURTUBI-FULL(علي بن أبي طالب والأوزاعي وعطاء والشعبي وأحمد بن حنبل وإسحاق بن راهويه); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(علي وعطاء والشعبي ومكحول والأوزاعي وأحمد وإسحاق وداود والطبري والشافعي,عمار); DURR-FULL(أبو هريرة); TAB-FULL(أبو مالك,ابن عمر,الشعبي,عكرمة,مكحول); THALABI(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم,رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم,سعيد بن المسيب، والأوزاعي، وأحمد، وإسحاق,علي); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الشعبي)+]
- 4:43/q35/p5 To mid-forearm — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (الأعمش): A report from al-Aʿmash says the Prophet wiped to halfway up the forearms; Ibn ʿAtiyya knows of no scholar who followed this report. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الأعمش): A report from al-A'mash in Abu Dawud's Musannaf says the Prophet wiped to the middle of his forearms; Ibn Atiyya says he knew of no one who followed this report. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم): A report in Abū Dāwūd says the Prophet wiped to the middle of his forearms; Ibn ʿAṭiyya says no one followed that report as far as he knew. [3 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(الأعمش); QURTUBI-FULL(الأعمش); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم)]

## 4:43/q36 How long does one tayammum last?  [فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q36/p1 Renew tayammum for each prayer — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (شريك بن عبد الله القاضي): Sharik ibn Abd Allah al-Qadi requires a new tayammum for every prayer, voluntary or obligatory. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن القاسم): One transmission from Ibn al-Qasim says the second of two obligatory prayers performed with one tayammum must be repeated while its time remains. IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (مالك): The well-known transmission from Ibn al-Qāsim says tayammum permits prayer without removing impurity, so each tayammum covers only one obligatory prayer; after a nullifier following tayammum for janāba, one tayammums again for wudu. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p93/r3) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p93/r4) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p93/r5) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p93/r6) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p95/r1) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p95/r2) supports this position. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p318/r2) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p229/r1) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p229/r2) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p229/r3) supports this position. [22 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(ابن القاسم,شريك بن عبد الله القاضي); IBNASHUR-FULL(مالك); TAB-FULL(آخرون,ابن عمر,الحسن,الشعبي,النخعي,عطاء,علي,قتادة,يحيى بن سعيد وعبد الكريم بن ربيعة); THALABI(سعيد بن المسيب والحسن والثوري وأبو عبيدة); BAGHAWI-FULL(سعيد بن المسيب والحسن والزهري والثوري وأصحاب الرأي,علي وابن عباس وابن عمر والشعبي والنخعي وقتادة ومالك والشافعي وأحمد وإسحاق)]
- 4:43/q36/p2 One tayammum serves multiple prayers — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة): Abu Hanifa, al-Thawri, al-Layth, Hasan ibn Hayy, and Dawud allow as many prayers as desired with one tayammum until ritual impurity occurs. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو حنيفة)]

## 4:43/q37 What happens when water is found after tayammum?  [فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q37/p1 A valid completed prayer need not be repeated — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (الشافعي وأبو حنيفة ومالك): Al-Shāfiʿī places tayammum at time's start, Abū Ḥanīfa and others at its end, while Mālik distinguishes the hopeless, hopeful, and uninformed about finding water. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن حبيب): When the chances of finding water or not finding it are equal, delay prayer until the end of the chosen time because water's agreed virtue outweighs the disputed virtue of praying early; Ibn Habib identifies the endpoint. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): If water appears during prayer, Malik says not to interrupt: complete the prayer and use water for later prayers; al-Shafi'i and Ibn al-Mundhir agree, while Abu Hanifa, Ahmad, al-Muzani, and others require stopping and restarting. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): There is agreement that finding water before beginning prayer invalidates tayammum and requires using the water. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi cites “do not invalidate your works” as support for completing a prayer begun validly with tayammum; no Sunna or consensus requires cutting it when water appears. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (أبو ذر): Abu Dharr reports the Prophet saying clean earth purifies a Muslim even without water for ten years; when water is found, one should use it on the skin, which is better. IBNABIHATIM (source note IBNABIHATIM:v3p962/r6) supports this position. [7 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(الشافعي وأبو حنيفة ومالك); QURTUBI-FULL(ابن حبيب,مالك); IBNKATHIR-FULL(أبو ذر); IBNABIHATIM(قتادة); QURTUBI-FULL+]
- 4:43/q37/p2 Repeat the prayer when water appears — TABRISI, d. 548 (أبو علي الجبائي): Al-Jubbaʾi says they must repeat a prayer performed while intoxicated. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن عمر): Ibn ʿUmar reports that the Prophet delayed ʿIsha while worshippers repeatedly slept and woke, then said only they were waiting for prayer; al-Qurtubi calls it the strongest report by chain and practice. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (عبد الملك بن الماجشون): Abd al-Malik ibn al-Majishun says that if water is found later, the worshipper must repeat the prayer without time limitation. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (طاووس): Tawus, Ata, al-Qasim ibn Muhammad, Makhul, Ibn Sirin, al-Zuhri, and Rabi'a all say that a person who finds water after the prayer repeats it. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك وابن نافع): Malik says one strike suffices, whereas Ibn Nafi requires repeating the prayer forever. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الليث والشافعي): Al-Layth and another report from al-Shāfiʿī allow tayammum and prayer when time would expire, but require repeating after water is found. [6 notes: TABRISI(أبو علي الجبائي); QURTUBI-FULL(ابن عمر,طاووس,عبد الملك بن الماجشون,مالك وابن نافع); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الليث والشافعي)]

## 4:43/q38 Can tayammum substitute for washing after major impurity?  [جُنُبًا · law]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q38/p1 Yes, tayammum substitutes for ghusl — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو عمر): Abu ʿUmar says no leading jurist followed ʿUmar and Ibn Masʿud here, likely because reports from ʿAmmar, ʿImran b. Husayn, and Abu Dharr allow a junub person to tayammum. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): The wording permits a junub without water to pray after tayammum, but tayammum does not end janāba because the person remains called junub. DURR-FULL, d. 911 (الأسلع بن شريك): In another report, Aslaʿ says Gabriel brought the tayammum verse when he told the Prophet he was junub; the Prophet demonstrated striking the ground, wiping the face, striking again, and wiping both sides of the arms. IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (الشافعي): Al-Shāfiʿī treats tayammum as a necessity-based permission, not removal of ritual impurity: a person who tayammums remains junub and may pray only as necessity permits, so the exception is connected. TABATABAI, d. 1402 (author): He defers detailed discussion of the junub and tayammum rulings to the commentary on al-Māʾida 5:6. | mentions 5:6 MUQATIL (source note MUQATIL:4:43/r9) supports this position. ABDURRAZZAQ (source note ABDURRAZZAQ:v1p163/r2) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p90/r8) supports this position. - 4:43/q38/p2 No, the junub must wait for water — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (أبو موسى الأشعري وابن مسعود): In a debate recorded by al-Bukhari, Abu Musa asks Ibn Masud what a junub person without water should do, citing Ammar's instruction and the tayammum verse; Ibn Masud first answers that he must wait for water.
