## 4:43/q39 What is the force of the preposition in wiping?  [بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُمْ · grammar]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q39/p1 It governs wiping without changing the object — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): The preposition باء may govern the verb or be absent without change of meaning; Sībawayh gives مسحت رأسه and مسحت برأسه, and the same alternation with the chest. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الزمخشري): Al-Zamakhsharī asks how the al-Māʾida phrase فامسحوا بوجوهكم وأيديكم منه can require part of the earth when bare rock has no soil, and answers that من marks a starting point. SAMIN-DURR, d. 756 (author): The object being wiped is omitted here and stated in 5:6 with منه, so this wording is understood in light of that verse. | mentions 5:6 SAMIN-DURR, d. 756 (سيبويه): Alternatively, باء is transitive: Sibawayh records مسحت رأسه وبرأسه, like نصحته ونصحت له. BURHAN, d. 794 (author): The al-Māʾida wording adds منه after the phrase about wiping faces and hands; al-Nisāʾ does not. BURHAN, d. 794 (author): باء also appears with objects in examples such as بأيديكم, بالمودة, باسم ربك, and بأيكم المفتون; المفتون is a passive participle, not a verbal noun. | mentions 68:6, 76:6, 22:25, 23:20, 4:43 IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Using bāʾ with the wiped areas, in wudu and tayammum, signals thorough wiping and prevents extending the concession beyond what is needed. TABATABAI, d. 1402 (روايات من طرق أهل السنة): Several Sunni reports likewise say the verse preceded wine's prohibition; al-Ṭabāṭabāʾī allows that they may mean the prohibition of praying sluggishly. SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): Al-Ma'idah details the full ablution and tayammum rulings, so it adds “from it” for clarification; al-Nisa' gives only some rulings and aptly leaves the phrase out. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p547/r1) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p547/r2) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p547/r3) supports this position. [12 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الزمخشري); SAMIN-DURR(سيبويه); BURHAN; IBNASHUR-FULL; TABATABAI(روايات من طرق أهل السنة); SAMARRAI-ASRAR; KASHSHAF-FULL(أبو حنيفة,قالوا)]
- 4:43/q39/p2 The bāʾ is connective or emphatic — NASAFI-FULL, d. 710 (author): The preposition bāʾ in the wiping phrase is said to be extra. SAMIN-DURR, d. 756 (أبو البقاء): بوجوهكم attaches to امسحوا; Abu al-Baqa regards the preposition باء as extra. [2 notes: NASAFI-FULL; SAMIN-DURR(أبو البقاء)]

## 4:43/q40 What do God's names at the close convey?  [إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ عَفُوًّا غَفُورًا · interpretation]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q40/p1 The names explain the concession and ease — BAYDAWI-FULL, d. 685 (author): God’s forgiveness explains the eased rule and dispensation. NASAFI-FULL, d. 710 (author): God is ʿafuww by granting concessions and ease, and ghafūr by pardoning error and shortcoming. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الزمخشري): Al-Zamakhsharī takes the divine description as an allusion to legal concession and ease: God forgives and pardons, so He chooses ease over hardship. BIQAI-FULL, d. 885 (author): Ghafūr adds erasure of the sin; the author connects this to the tayammum concession, which makes prayer virtuous instead of leaving it or praying without purification. BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): God’s pardon and forgiveness explain and affirm this permission and ease: His constant practice is to pardon sinners, so He makes matters easier rather than harder. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): The divine names explain and affirm the dispensation: God's settled practice is to pardon those at fault and forgive sinners, so He provides ease. ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): Al-Alusi says the facilitation sense in the hadith is not certain, and it is unlikely that forgiveness here specifically concerns those who prayed intoxicated and their recitation. MUQATIL (source note MUQATIL:4:43/r12) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p97/r1) supports this position. TAB-FULL (source note TAB-FULL:v7p97/r2) supports this position. ZAJJAJ (source note ZAJJAJ:v2p55/r9) supports this position. IBNABIHATIM (source note IBNABIHATIM:v3p962/r7) supports this position. IBNABIHATIM (source note IBNABIHATIM:v3p962/r8) supports this position. JISHUMI (source note JISHUMI:v2p1567/r30) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p547/r4) supports this position. [15 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL; NASAFI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الزمخشري); BIQAI-FULL; BURSEVI; ALUSI-FULL; MUQATIL; TAB-FULL; ZAJJAJ; IBNABIHATIM(سعيد بن المسيب,سلمة بن وهرام); JISHUMI; KASHSHAF-FULL]
- 4:43/q40/p2 God pardons and covers sins — TABRISI, d. 548 (author): Ghafūr describes God as frequently covering the sins of His servants. TABRISI, d. 548 (قيل): An alternative gloss takes ʿafūw as abundant pardon and forbearance. IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (الخطابي): Al-Khaṭṭābī says ʿafuww is an intensive form and means pardoning sins and leaving the wrongdoer unpunished. IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (author): Another explanation derives the word from wind erasing a trace: the pardoner wipes away sins through pardon. QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): God has always accepted pardon and been gentle, covering sin’s punishment rather than punishing. ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): Abū Ḥayyān criticizes al-Zamakhsharī for accepting the apparent sense here despite usually distorting wording for his school, and for speaking of forgiveness without the repentance qualification he usually requires. IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (author): The Prophet may have ordered wudu and prayer as repentance, as in the report that a sinner who performs wudu and two rak‘as is forgiven, cited at Quran 3:135. | mentions 3:135 IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (author): God’s pardon includes legislating tayammum and allowing prayer with it when water is absent, as mercy, leniency, and relief. [8 notes: TABRISI(قيل); IBNJAWZI-ZAD(الخطابي); QURTUBI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL; IBNKATHIR-FULL]

## 4:43/q41 What wisdom do the prayer and purification rules express?  [* · interpretation]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q41/p1 the author offers a second explanation: the prophet may have specially exempted himself and then ʿalī, as he had other exclusive privileges — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The author offers a second explanation: the Prophet may have specially exempted himself and then ʿAlī, as he had other exclusive privileges. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p225/r2) supports this position. BAGHAWI-FULL (source note BAGHAWI-FULL:v2p225/r3) supports this position. [3 notes: QURTUBI-FULL; BAGHAWI-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم)]
- 4:43/q41/p2 al-bazzar transmits the clean-earth purification report from abu hurayra, and al-qattan authenticates it — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (أبو هريرة وأبو بكر البزار وأبو الحسن القطان): Al-Bazzar transmits the clean-earth purification report from Abu Hurayra, and al-Qattan authenticates it. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(أبو هريرة وأبو بكر البزار وأبو الحسن القطان)]
- 4:43/q41/p3 the terrifying judgment scene makes sinners wish for nonexistence and prevents concealment; because salvation requires purity through faith and obedience, the discussion turns to worldly prayer and purification — BIQAI-FULL, d. 885 (author): The terrifying judgment scene makes sinners wish for nonexistence and prevents concealment; because salvation requires purity through faith and obedience, the discussion turns to worldly prayer and purification. [1 notes: BIQAI-FULL]
- 4:43/q41/p4 saʿid ibn bashir says clean earth is earth that rain has fallen on and purified — DURR-FULL, d. 911 (سعيد بن بشير): Saʿid ibn Bashir says clean earth is earth that rain has fallen on and purified. [1 notes: DURR-FULL(سعيد بن بشير)]
- 4:43/q41/p5 the following discussion develops inner and outer purity: prayer follows faith, purification is prayer's first condition, and major ablution connects closely to marriage and family rules; both require a sound mind — ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): The following discussion develops inner and outer purity: prayer follows faith, purification is prayer's first condition, and major ablution connects closely to marriage and family rules; both require a sound mind. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 4:43/q41/p6 the prophet listed among five gifts unique to him that the earth was made a mosque and a purifier — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (جابر): The Prophet listed among five gifts unique to him that the earth was made a mosque and a purifier. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL(جابر)]
- 4:43/q41/p7 ibn ashur's proposed wisdom is to preserve the believer's sense that purification is obligatory and prayer is sacred, leaving no situation in which one approaches prayer without ritual preparation — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Ibn Ashur's proposed wisdom is to preserve the believer's sense that purification is obligatory and prayer is sacred, leaving no situation in which one approaches prayer without ritual preparation. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 4:43/q41/p8 because tayammum is symbolic purification, the law requires only the face and hands for both minor and major impurity — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Because tayammum is symbolic purification, the law requires only the face and hands for both minor and major impurity. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 4:43/q41/p9 junub means major ritual impurity and derives from a root meaning distance; the impure should keep distant from prayer and its places until purified, paralleling intoxication — STUDYQURAN (author): Junub means major ritual impurity and derives from a root meaning distance; the impure should keep distant from prayer and its places until purified, paralleling intoxication. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN]

## 4:43/q42 How does this verse connect with other verses or its sūra?  [* · links]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q42/p1 a second version says ʿabd al-raḥmān ibn ʿawf was the person they put forward to lead prayer and who mixed up the sūra — IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (علي بن أبي طالب): A second version says ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf was the person they put forward to lead prayer and who mixed up the sūra. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(علي بن أبي طالب)]
- 4:43/q42/p2 rāzī rejects the preceding interpretations as unsupported departures from the wording; reading this part as janāba would repeat the ruling already stated — RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (author): Rāzī rejects the preceding interpretations as unsupported departures from the wording; reading this part as janāba would repeat the ruling already stated. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p3 some identify the tayammum verse as the one in al-maʾida; abu ʿumar allows either al-maʾida's ablution verse or this verse — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Some identify the tayammum verse as the one in al-Maʾida; Abu ʿUmar allows either al-Maʾida's ablution verse or this verse. | mentions 5:6 [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p4 abu ʿumar says tayammum occurs only in the two medinan verses in al-maʾida and al-nisaʾ — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو عمر): Abu ʿUmar says tayammum occurs only in the two Medinan verses in al-Maʾida and al-Nisaʾ. | mentions 5:6 [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو عمر)]
- 4:43/q42/p5 al-qurtubi connects the command to the description of successful believers as humble in their prayers — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Al-Qurtubi connects the command to the description of successful believers as humble in their prayers. | mentions 23:1, 23:2 [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p6 the word tayammum occurs in al-baqara, this sura, and al-ma'ida; qurtubi identifies the present verse as the tayammum verse — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The word tayammum occurs in al-Baqara, this sura, and al-Ma'ida; Qurtubi identifies the present verse as the tayammum verse. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p7 abū ḥayyān says the qurʾān-recitation ruling has no exegetical connection to the wording at hand — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): Abū Ḥayyān says the Qurʾān-recitation ruling has no exegetical connection to the wording at hand. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p8 many tayammum rulings are treated in law books and al-māʾida; its qualification is applied to this verse and supports al-shāfiʿī's requirement that some material be transferred to the face and hands — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): Many tayammum rulings are treated in law books and al-Māʾida; its qualification is applied to this verse and supports al-Shāfiʿī's requirement that some material be transferred to the face and hands. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p9 a sound bukhari report says the prophet ordered every opening into the mosque closed except abu bakr's; ibn kathir links this late command to abu bakr's future leadership and need for mosque access — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم): A sound Bukhari report says the Prophet ordered every opening into the mosque closed except Abu Bakr's; Ibn Kathir links this late command to Abu Bakr's future leadership and need for mosque access. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم)]
- 4:43/q42/p10 the citation from 4:43 is one of the examples linked to the preceding claim about using a word in literal and figurative senses — BIQAI-FULL, d. 885 (author): The citation from 4:43 is one of the examples linked to the preceding claim about using a word in literal and figurative senses. | mentions 4:43 [1 notes: BIQAI-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p11 the prohibition purifies prayer, the chief act of worship, from blemish so believers combine sincere devotion to god with good conduct toward others, connecting this verse to the preceding discussion — ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): The prohibition purifies prayer, the chief act of worship, from blemish so believers combine sincere devotion to God with good conduct toward others, connecting this verse to the preceding discussion. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p12 since the preceding passage forbade associating partners with god, this passage also forbids an unsuspected route that could lead to it — ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (author): Since the preceding passage forbade associating partners with God, this passage also forbids an unsuspected route that could lead to it. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 4:43/q42/p13 the exception indicates generally that the following case differs from the preceding rule, but does not by itself establish which individual cases are exempt or their legal status — ALUSI-FULL, d. 1270 (شيخ الإسلام): The exception indicates generally that the following case differs from the preceding rule, but does not by itself establish which individual cases are exempt or their legal status. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(شيخ الإسلام)]
- 4:43/q42/p14 elmalılı characterizes the hanafi approach as lenient and the shafi'i approach as following stricter precaution — ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): Elmalılı characterizes the Hanafi approach as lenient and the Shafi'i approach as following stricter precaution. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 4:43/q42/p15 al-qurṭubī firmly identifies the verse revealed at al-muraysīʿ as this verse in al-nisa, since al-maʾida’s verse is called the verse of ablution — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (القرطبي): Al-Qurṭubī firmly identifies the verse revealed at al-Muraysīʿ as this verse in al-Nisa, since al-Maʾida’s verse is called the verse of ablution. | mentions 5:6 [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL(القرطبي)]
- 4:43/q42/p16 although the order of al-nisa and al-maʾida is disputed, ibn ʿashur prefers the view that al-nisa was revealed first — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Although the order of al-Nisa and al-Maʾida is disputed, Ibn ʿAshur prefers the view that al-Nisa was revealed first. | mentions 5:6 [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL+]
- 4:43/q42/p17 abū ḥanīfa treats tayammum as removing ritual impurity until invalidated, so the exception is disconnected; after tayammum for janāba, a later nullifier requires wudu. ibn ʿashur calls this the apparent view, with no sunna evidence — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (أبو حنيفة): Abū Ḥanīfa treats tayammum as removing ritual impurity until invalidated, so the exception is disconnected; after tayammum for janāba, a later nullifier requires wudu. Ibn ʿAshur calls this the apparent view, with no Sunna evidence against it. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL(أبو حنيفة)+]
- 4:43/q42/p18 there is no need to force a link to adjacent verses: the qur'an descended in installments, and coherence is required only for passages revealed together or clearly connected — TABATABAI, d. 1402 (author): There is no need to force a link to adjacent verses: the Qur'an descended in installments, and coherence is required only for passages revealed together or clearly connected. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 4:43/q42/p19 he also considers an alternate sequence: al-naḥl, al-aʿrāf, al-baqara, al-nisāʾ, then al-māʾida, implying that the prayer restriction followed the prohibition in al-baqara — TABATABAI, d. 1402 (author): He also considers an alternate sequence: al-Naḥl, al-Aʿrāf, al-Baqara, al-Nisāʾ, then al-Māʾida, implying that the prayer restriction followed the prohibition in al-Baqara. | mentions 16:67, 7:33, 2:219, 5:90 [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 4:43/q42/p20 an apparent insertion can occur when verses with one connected context descend gradually and another verse is sent down as a need arises; it may clarify a point rather than be unrelated — TABATABAI, d. 1402 (author): An apparent insertion can occur when verses with one connected context descend gradually and another verse is sent down as a need arises; it may clarify a point rather than be unrelated. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 4:43/q42/p21 al-kirmani likewise says al-nisa' mentions only some ablution and tayammum rulings, making omission apt, while al-ma'idah gives all the rulings and therefore states the phrase — SAMARRAI-ASRAR (الكرماني): Al-Kirmani likewise says al-Nisa' mentions only some ablution and tayammum rulings, making omission apt, while al-Ma'idah gives all the rulings and therefore states the phrase. ## 4:43/q43 What inward reading is given to the verse?  [* · inward]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q43/p1 washing means cleansing attachment to the lower realm through repentance, seeking forgiveness, and regret — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Washing means cleansing attachment to the lower realm through repentance, seeking forgiveness, and regret. SULAMI (source note SULAMI:v1p149/r1) supports this position. SULAMI (source note SULAMI:v1p149/r2) supports this position. [3 notes: QASHANI; SULAMI(الواسطي,بعضهم)]
- 4:43/q43/p2 illness is false belief and deadly vice of the heart; travel is wandering in ignorance and confusion while greed seeks selfish pleasure and impure substance — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Illness is false belief and deadly vice of the heart; travel is wandering in ignorance and confusion while greed seeks selfish pleasure and impure substance. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p3 travel signifies wandering in ignorance and confusion to seek the soul's pleasure and impure matter through greed — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Travel signifies wandering in ignorance and confusion to seek the soul's pleasure and impure matter through greed. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p4 coming from the privy signifies engaging in tainted wealth and acquisition, with a lasting imprint of love and inclination — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Coming from the privy signifies engaging in tainted wealth and acquisition, with a lasting imprint of love and inclination. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p5 touching women signifies attachment to souls and direct engagement in their pleasures and desires — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Touching women signifies attachment to souls and direct engagement in their pleasures and desires. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p6 lacking water means lacking knowledge that guides release from these states and purifies from them — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Lacking water means lacking knowledge that guides release from these states and purifies from them. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p7 tayammum means turning toward pure preparedness and returning to the original innate disposition — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Tayammum means turning toward pure preparedness and returning to the original innate disposition. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p8 wiping means erasing acquired lower dispositions from the self and its descended attributes; the soil removes their traces and restores purity — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Wiping means erasing acquired lower dispositions from the self and its descended attributes; the soil removes their traces and restores purity. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p9 god's pardon removes dark, entrenched dispositions when abandoned, clearing preparedness for encounter; his forgiveness veils creatures' attributes and essences with his own — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): God's pardon removes dark, entrenched dispositions when abandoned, clearing preparedness for encounter; His forgiveness veils creatures' attributes and essences with His own. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p10 the address is to those with intellectual faith; a believer with direct faith would not be heedless in prayer — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): The address is to those with intellectual faith; a believer with direct faith would not be heedless in prayer. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p11 prayer means the station of presence and intimate conversation with god — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Prayer means the station of presence and intimate conversation with God. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p12 drunkenness means sleep of heedlessness or intoxication with caprice and love of the world — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Drunkenness means sleep of heedlessness or intoxication with caprice and love of the world. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p13 before praying, know what you say and keep the heart from worldly occupations and distracting whispers — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Before praying, know what you say and keep the heart from worldly occupations and distracting whispers. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p14 sexual impurity means distance from truth through attachment to the soul and its pleasures; passing through means meeting only necessary needs, not being wholly absorbed in them — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Sexual impurity means distance from truth through attachment to the soul and its pleasures; passing through means meeting only necessary needs, not being wholly absorbed in them. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p15 passing through worldly pleasures is limited to necessity and benefit, such as food, clothing, and procreation, to avoid being imprinted by total absorption — QASHANI, d. 736 (author): Passing through worldly pleasures is limited to necessity and benefit, such as food, clothing, and procreation, to avoid being imprinted by total absorption. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 4:43/q43/p16 the inward warning forbids approaching prayer intoxicated by heedlessness and desire: anything that distracts the heart from god is a kind of drunkenness — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): The inward warning forbids approaching prayer intoxicated by heedlessness and desire: anything that distracts the heart from God is a kind of drunkenness. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p17 there is drunkenness from wine, from heedlessness caused by worldly love, and from the self; the hardest is self-intoxication, whose consequence is true separation — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): There is drunkenness from wine, from heedlessness caused by worldly love, and from the self; the hardest is self-intoxication, whose consequence is true separation. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p18 the persian poem depicts the self as its own trap and urges leaving selfhood and heedlessness to attain presence and union — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): The Persian poem depicts the self as its own trap and urges leaving selfhood and heedlessness to attain presence and union. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p19 at the opening takbir, allahu akbar means that god is greater and more exalted than everything — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): At the opening takbir, Allahu akbar means that God is greater and more exalted than everything. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p20 spiritual travel is wavering among pursuit of this world, the afterlife, and the lord — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Spiritual travel is wavering among pursuit of this world, the afterlife, and the Lord. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p21 coming from the privy signifies following one's caprice — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Coming from the privy signifies following one's caprice. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p22 touching women signifies engagement in worldly occupations, which estranges the seeker from god after nearness to the sacred precincts and gardens of intimacy — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Touching women signifies engagement in worldly occupations, which estranges the seeker from God after nearness to the sacred precincts and gardens of intimacy. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p23 lacking water signifies inability to repent and return sincerely to god by turning away from created beings — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Lacking water signifies inability to repent and return sincerely to God by turning away from created beings. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p24 the pure earth is the dust of the feet of virtuous spiritual masters, cleansed of bad character and deeds — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): The pure earth is the dust of the feet of virtuous spiritual masters, cleansed of bad character and deeds. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p25 wiping the face and hands means taking the masters' dust and clinging to their generous hems, submitting sincerely to their guidance — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Wiping the face and hands means taking the masters' dust and clinging to their generous hems, submitting sincerely to their guidance. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p26 god may pardon sectarian attachment and failure to turn wholly to him, and may pardon worldly contamination through this praiseworthy spiritual discipline — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): God may pardon sectarian attachment and failure to turn wholly to Him, and may pardon worldly contamination through this praiseworthy spiritual discipline. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p27 god forgives traces of misery left by desire's dust; those who keep company with the virtuous attain felicity, for their companions do not suffer — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): God forgives traces of misery left by desire's dust; those who keep company with the virtuous attain felicity, for their companions do not suffer. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p28 true takbir leaves no other greatness, remembrance, love, or desire in the heart alongside god; otherwise the speaker's state contradicts the words — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): True takbir leaves no other greatness, remembrance, love, or desire in the heart alongside God; otherwise the speaker's state contradicts the words. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p29 spiritual intoxication is heedlessness, which prevents nearness to god just as literal drunkenness prevents understanding prayer — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Spiritual intoxication is heedlessness, which prevents nearness to God just as literal drunkenness prevents understanding prayer. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p30 nearness is conditioned on prostration, as the command to prostrate and draw near states — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Nearness is conditioned on prostration, as the command to prostrate and draw near states. | mentions 96:19 [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p31 prostration means descending from the mount of one's existent attributes, carried by divine generosity to the threshold of divine being and a vision of beauty and majesty; this is the secret of sitting for tashahhud — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Prostration means descending from the mount of one's existent attributes, carried by divine generosity to the threshold of divine being and a vision of beauty and majesty; this is the secret of sitting for tashahhud after prostration. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p32 spiritual major impurity is attachment to the lowly world, which makes the seeker deserving of distance from god — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Spiritual major impurity is attachment to the lowly world, which makes the seeker deserving of distance from God. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p33 one may cross worldly concerns only by the outward path of sacred law, taking food and drink to preserve life, clothing for protection and modesty, and sex to preserve offspring — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): One may cross worldly concerns only by the outward path of sacred law, taking food and drink to preserve life, clothing for protection and modesty, and sex to preserve offspring. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p34 spiritual washing is achieved through nearness, repentance, sincere seeking, good intention, and purity of purpose from worldly attachment and desire — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Spiritual washing is achieved through nearness, repentance, sincere seeking, good intention, and purity of purpose from worldly attachment and desire. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 4:43/q43/p35 spiritual illness is a heart whose disposition has become crooked in seeking the real — BURSEVI, d. 1127 (author): Spiritual illness is a heart whose disposition has become crooked in seeking the Real. [1 notes: BURSEVI]

- 4:43/q43/p37 the concession teaches that inward purity is foundational, but outward cleanliness also matters; neither can replace the other — ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): The concession teaches that inward purity is foundational, but outward cleanliness also matters; neither can replace the other. - 4:43/q43/p38 in necessity, tayammum is primarily an inward act that preserves the material condition and outward form as fully as possible — ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): In necessity, tayammum is primarily an inward act that preserves the material condition and outward form as fully as possible. - 4:43/q43/p39 the discussion moves from sound mind and bodily and spiritual purity toward intellectual and moral education, then draws attention to the conduct of misguided aggressors — ELMALILI, d. 1361 (author): The discussion moves from sound mind and bodily and spiritual purity toward intellectual and moral education, then draws attention to the conduct of misguided aggressors. - 4:43/q43/p40 ghusl provides cleanliness and joins bodily completeness in prayer to inward completeness in communion and humility before god — IBNASHUR-FULL, d. 1393 (author): Ghusl provides cleanliness and joins bodily completeness in prayer to inward completeness in communion and humility before God. ## 4:43/q44 What other readings or phonetic details are reported?  [* · readings]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q44/p1 al-tabrisi prefers the mosque-entry interpretation: the ending already gives the junub’s rule when water is unavailable, so reading the opening as prayer would repeat that rule — TABRISI, d. 548 (author): Al-Tabrisi prefers the mosque-entry interpretation: the ending already gives the junub’s rule when water is unavailable, so reading the opening as prayer would repeat that rule. THALABI (source note THALABI:v3p313/r2) supports this position. JISHUMI (source note JISHUMI:v2p1565/r2) supports this position. JISHUMI (source note JISHUMI:v2p1565/r3) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p545#2/r5) supports this position. KASHSHAF-FULL (source note KASHSHAF-FULL:v1p545#2/r6) supports this position. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p56#2/r5) supports this position. IBNATIYYA-FULL (source note IBNATIYYA-FULL:v2p56#2/r6) supports this position. [8 notes: THALABI; JISHUMI(النخعي); KASHSHAF-FULL; IBNATIYYA-FULL(أبو الفتح,فرقة والأعمش والنخعي); TABRISI+]
- 4:43/q44/p2 ibrāhīm al-nakhaʿī says the verse came down after the prophet's companions suffered spreading wounds while becoming junub and brought their difficulty to him — IBNJAWZI-ZAD, d. 597 (إبراهيم النخعي): Ibrāhīm al-Nakhaʿī says the verse came down after the Prophet's companions suffered spreading wounds while becoming junub and brought their difficulty to him. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(إبراهيم النخعي)]
- 4:43/q44/p3 if the wayfarer means a junub traveler, the alternative reading requires unstated conditions about having water or needing tayammum; the mosque reading requires no such additions — RAZI-FULL, d. 606 (أصحاب الشافعي): If the wayfarer means a junub traveler, the alternative reading requires unstated conditions about having water or needing tayammum; the mosque reading requires no such additions. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(أصحاب الشافعي)+]
- 4:43/q44/p4 the use of 'prayers' for places of prayer appears in 22:40; the exception for a junub person passing through supports the mosque reading because it permits passage, not prayer there — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The use of 'prayers' for places of prayer appears in 22:40; the exception for a junub person passing through supports the mosque reading because it permits passage, not prayer there. | mentions 22:40 [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q44/p5 the form is read sukārā with fatḥa; another form may be a plural like halkā, a singular for an intoxicated people, or a collective adjective like ḥublā — BAYDAWI-FULL, d. 685 (author): The form is read sukārā with fatḥa; another form may be a plural like halkā, a singular for an intoxicated people, or a collective adjective like ḥublā. [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 4:43/q44/p6 for a junub person, prayer means the prayer itself; a resident must wash, while a traveler without water may use dry ablution, which is the hanafi reading also transmitted from ali — NASAFI-FULL, d. 710 (أبو حنيفة): For a junub person, prayer means the prayer itself; a resident must wash, while a traveler without water may use dry ablution, which is the Hanafi reading also transmitted from Ali. [1 notes: NASAFI-FULL(أبو حنيفة)+]
- 4:43/q44/p7 the places-of-prayer interpretation is supported by reading ولا جنبا إلا عابري سبيل as referring to mosques — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (قيل): The places-of-prayer interpretation is supported by reading ولا جنبا إلا عابري سبيل as referring to mosques. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(قيل)]
- 4:43/q44/p8 al-aʿmash reads سكرى with ḍamma on س, on the pattern حبلى, as an adjective of a collective group — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الأعمش): Al-Aʿmash reads سكرى with ḍamma on س, on the pattern حبلى, as an adjective of a collective group. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الأعمش)]

## 4:43/q45 What further question about this verse is addressed?  [* · interpretation]
turns on: The cited interpretations, reports, language arguments, and legal evidence differ.
- 4:43/q45/p1 in religious usage, tayammum became the established name for the known act of worship — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (author): In religious usage, tayammum became the established name for the known act of worship. MUJAHID (source note MUJAHID:v1p1/r1) supports this position. [2 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL; MUJAHID(أصحاب رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم)]
- 4:43/q45/p2 al-khalil and others explain ṣaʿīd as the face or surface of the earth; dhu al-rumma’s verse supplies poetic evidence for the term — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (الخليل وغيره): Al-Khalil and others explain ṣaʿīd as the face or surface of the earth; Dhu al-Rumma’s verse supplies poetic evidence for the term. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(الخليل وغيره)]
- 4:43/q45/p3 malik has two positions in the mudawwana: perform tayammum without repeating, or repeat; in other works he says to obtain water and wash even after sunrise — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (مالك): Malik has two positions in the Mudawwana: perform tayammum without repeating, or repeat; in other works he says to obtain water and wash even after sunrise. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(مالك)]

- 4:43/q45/p6 the majority follows the qurʾanic order by wiping the face before the hands; some routes of ʿammar’s hadith in al-bukhari put the hands first, as do scholars reasoning by analogy with reversing ablution order — IBNATIYYA-FULL, d. 542 (الجمهور): The majority follows the Qurʾanic order by wiping the face before the hands; some routes of ʿAmmar’s hadith in al-Bukhari put the hands first, as do scholars reasoning by analogy with reversing ablution order. - 4:43/q45/p61 the necklace loss occurred during the banu al-mustaliq raid, the same expedition also called al-muraysiʿ — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The necklace loss occurred during the Banu al-Mustaliq raid, the same expedition also called al-Muraysiʿ. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p62 ʿamr b. al-ʿas performed tayammum when severe cold made bathing dangerous, and the prophet did not order him to wash or repeat — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (عمرو بن العاص): ʿAmr b. al-ʿAs performed tayammum when severe cold made bathing dangerous, and the Prophet did not order him to wash or repeat. - 4:43/q45/p63 a stricter view attributed to al-shafiʿi allows tayammum only when death is feared; qadi abu al-hasan also reports this from malik — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): A stricter view attributed to al-Shafiʿi allows tayammum only when death is feared; Qadi Abu al-Hasan also reports this from Malik. - 4:43/q45/p64 khalifa b. khayyat and abu ʿumar date the raid to shaʿban 6 ah; abu dharr or namilah b. ʿabd allah is named as medina's acting governor — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Khalifa b. - 4:43/q45/p65 the prophet surprised banu al-mustaliq at al-muraysiʿ while they were unaware, taking captives; their battle cry was amit amit — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The Prophet surprised Banu al-Mustaliq at al-Muraysiʿ while they were unaware, taking captives; their battle cry was Amit amit. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p66 another account says banu al-mustaliq had assembled to attack the prophet, who went out to meet them after hearing of it — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Another account says Banu al-Mustaliq had assembled to attack the Prophet, who went out to meet them after hearing of it. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p67 illness is the body's departure from its ordinary, balanced condition; it may be severe or slight — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Illness is the body's departure from its ordinary, balanced condition; it may be severe or slight. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p68 the other maliki view bars a tayammum imam from leading people with ablution because the imam should have the higher ritual rank — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The other Maliki view bars a tayammum imam from leading people with ablution because the imam should have the higher ritual rank. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p69 a report from jabir says a tayammum imam must not lead worshippers who have ablution; al-daraqutni grades its chain weak — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (جابر بن عبد الله): A report from Jabir says a tayammum imam must not lead worshippers who have ablution; al-Daraqutni grades its chain weak. - 4:43/q45/p70 al-daraqutni says the jabir wound report is weak through al-zubayr b. khurayq; al-awzaʿi's ibn ʿabbas route is the sounder version, while another route is mursal. ibn abi hatim records a musnad route — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الدارقطني): Al-Daraqutni says the Jabir wound report is weak through al-Zubayr b. - 4:43/q45/p71 those who exclude healthy residents argue that the text conditioned this concession on illness or travel, like fasting exemption and shortened prayer — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Those who exclude healthy residents argue that the text conditioned this concession on illness or travel, like fasting exemption and shortened prayer. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p72 al-zuhri reads al-ghayt; al-qurtubi says it may be a lightened form of al-ghayt or a y-for-waw reversal from al-ghawt — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الزهري): Al-Zuhri reads al-ghayt; al-Qurtubi says it may be a lightened form of al-ghayt or a y-for-waw reversal from al-ghawt. - 4:43/q45/p73 ahmad says light sleep does not matter if it neither penetrates nor overwhelms the heart — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أحمد بن حنبل): Ahmad says light sleep does not matter if it neither penetrates nor overwhelms the heart. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أحمد بن حنبل)]
- 4:43/q45/p74 abu hanifa and his companions require ablution only for someone sleeping on a side or sitting with weight shifted — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة وأصحابه): Abu Hanifa and his companions require ablution only for someone sleeping on a side or sitting with weight shifted. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو حنيفة وأصحابه)]
- 4:43/q45/p75 al-shafiʿi says sitting sleep does not invalidate ablution; ibn wahb also transmits this from malik — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafiʿi says sitting sleep does not invalidate ablution; Ibn Wahb also transmits this from Malik. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الشافعي)]
- 4:43/q45/p76 al-qurtubi considers malik's well-known position the soundest of these views — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): Al-Qurtubi considers Malik's well-known position the soundest of these views. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(مالك)+]
- 4:43/q45/p77 muslims agree that fainting, insanity, and intoxication, as losses of reason, require ablution — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Muslims agree that fainting, insanity, and intoxication, as losses of reason, require ablution. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p78 sleep has three positions: it is itself an invalidator, never an invalidator without another event, or a middle case based on its depth or posture — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Sleep has three positions: it is itself an invalidator, never an invalidator without another event, or a middle case based on its depth or posture. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p79 al-muzani treats any amount of sleep as an invalidator; this is implied by malik's muwatta wording and by the safwan report — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (المزني أبو إبراهيم إسماعيل): Al-Muzani treats any amount of sleep as an invalidator; this is implied by Malik's Muwatta wording and by the Safwan report. - 4:43/q45/p80 safwan reports the prophet allowed wiping over the khuffs after urine, stool, or sleep, removing them only for janaba; al-qurtubi says al-tirmidhi authenticated the report — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (صفوان بن عسال المرادي): Safwan reports the Prophet allowed wiping over the khuffs after urine, stool, or sleep, removing them only for janaba; al-Qurtubi says al-Tirmidhi authenticated the report. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(صفوان بن عسال المرادي)]
- 4:43/q45/p81 proponents equate sleep with urine and stool because deep sleep overwhelms reason; they also cite ali's general report that sleep loosens the anal tether and requires ablution — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Proponents equate sleep with urine and stool because deep sleep overwhelms reason; they also cite Ali's general report that sleep loosens the anal tether and requires ablution. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p82 abu musa regarded sleep as no invalidator at all unless another event occurred; he would sleep under guard and pray on waking, as did ʿubayda, saʿid b. al-musayyab, and al-awzaʿi in one report — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو موسى الأشعري): Abu Musa regarded sleep as no invalidator at all unless another event occurred; he would sleep under guard and pray on waking, as did ʿUbayda, Saʿid b. al-Musayyab, and al-Awzaʿi in one report. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو موسى الأشعري)]
- 4:43/q45/p83 malik requires ablution after deep, prolonged sleep in any posture; al-zuhri, rabiʿa, and al-awzaʿi in one narration agree — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (مالك): Malik requires ablution after deep, prolonged sleep in any posture; al-Zuhri, Rabiʿa, and al-Awzaʿi in one narration agree. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(مالك)]
- 4:43/q45/p84 al-qurtubi interprets malik's and safwan's references to sleep as heavy sleep that dominates the sleeper, in light of ibn ʿumar's report — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Al-Qurtubi interprets Malik's and Safwan's references to sleep as heavy sleep that dominates the sleeper, in light of Ibn ʿUmar's report. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p85 wakiʿ's safwan route says or wind instead of or sleep; daraqutni says no one else transmits that wording, and wakiʿ is trustworthy, weakening the use of safwan as proof that sleep itself invalidates ablution — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (وكيع): Wakiʿ's Safwan route says or wind instead of or sleep; Daraqutni says no one else transmits that wording, and Wakiʿ is trustworthy, weakening the use of Safwan as proof that sleep itself invalidates ablution. - 4:43/q45/p86 a report from ibn ʿabbas says the prophet slept prostrating, then said only side-sleep relaxes the joints and requires ablution; al-daraqutni, abu dawud, and abu ʿumar call it unsound or munkar — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن عباس): A report from Ibn ʿAbbas says the Prophet slept prostrating, then said only side-sleep relaxes the joints and requires ablution; al-Daraqutni, Abu Dawud, and Abu ʿUmar call it unsound or munkar. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 4:43/q45/p87 al-shafiʿi, al-tabari, and dawud require ablution after sleep in any position except sitting, which is unlikely to become deep; this is also reported from ali, ibn masʿud, and ibn ʿumar — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafiʿi, al-Tabari, and Dawud require ablution after sleep in any position except sitting, which is unlikely to become deep; this is also reported from Ali, Ibn Masʿud, and Ibn ʿUmar. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الشافعي)]
- 4:43/q45/p88 ʿamr b. shuʿayb transmits a prophetic report distinguishing sitting sleep, which does not require ablution, from lying on one's side, which does — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (عمرو بن شعيب): ʿAmr b. Shuʿayb transmits a prophetic report distinguishing sitting sleep, which does not require ablution, from lying on one's side, which does. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(عمرو بن شعيب)]
- 4:43/q45/p89 the prophet directed worshippers to begin eating when dinner is served at the time prayer is called, removing a distraction so they can approach worship with an unoccupied, humble heart — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم): The Prophet directed worshippers to begin eating when dinner is served at the time prayer is called, removing a distraction so they can approach worship with an unoccupied, humble heart. - 4:43/q45/p91 al-shafi'i takes 'prayer' to mean its place, the mosque, with an omitted word understood — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i takes 'prayer' to mean its place, the mosque, with an omitted word understood. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الشافعي)]
- 4:43/q45/p92 ubayda al-salmani interprets the phrase as being in need of urination, supported by the prohibition on praying while needing to urinate or while squeezing one's thighs — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (عبيدة السلماني): Ubayda al-Salmani interprets the phrase as being in need of urination, supported by the prohibition on praying while needing to urinate or while squeezing one's thighs. - 4:43/q45/p93 the report says a person who sleeps sitting does not need ablution, but one who lies down does — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (عمرو بن شعيب): The report says a person who sleeps sitting does not need ablution, but one who lies down does. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(عمرو بن شعيب)]

- 4:43/q45/p95 malik does not make this unusual outlet obligatorily invalidate ablution, unlike al-shafiʿi; this supports malik's distinction from the hanafi rule based on impurity alone — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Malik does not make this unusual outlet obligatorily invalidate ablution, unlike al-Shafiʿi; this supports Malik's distinction from the Hanafi rule based on impurity alone. - 4:43/q45/p96 al-shafiʿi requires ablution when any part of a man's body contacts a woman's body without a barrier, regardless of desire; this is also attributed to ibn masʿud, ibn ʿumar, al-zuhri, and rabiʿa — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafiʿi requires ablution when any part of a man's body contacts a woman's body without a barrier, regardless of desire; this is also attributed to Ibn Masʿud, Ibn ʿUmar, al-Zuhri, and Rabiʿa. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p98 al-marwazi says only malik and al-layth require ablution when pleasure comes through clothing; al-qurtubi adds that ibn ʿabd al-barr also attributes this rule to ishaq and ahmad and reports it from other early authorities — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (المروزي): Al-Marwazi says only Malik and al-Layth require ablution when pleasure comes through clothing; al-Qurtubi adds that Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr also attributes this rule to Ishaq and Ahmad and reports it from other early authorities. - 4:43/q45/p100 al-qurtubi says ghamz is pressing with the hand, like feeling a ram to test whether it is fat, not striking someone with a sleeve — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Al-Qurtubi says ghamz is pressing with the hand, like feeling a ram to test whether it is fat, not striking someone with a sleeve. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p101 aisha describes the prophet moving her legs when he prostrated and her extending them again when he stood; qurtubi treats this as direct contact without an intervening barrier — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (عائشة): Aisha describes the Prophet moving her legs when he prostrated and her extending them again when he stood; Qurtubi treats this as direct contact without an intervening barrier. - 4:43/q45/p102 al-muzani proposes that a barrier covered the prophet's foot; qurtubi rejects this because the default is direct contact unless a barrier is established — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (المزني): Al-Muzani proposes that a barrier covered the Prophet's foot; Qurtubi rejects this because the default is direct contact unless a barrier is established. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(المزني)]
- 4:43/q45/p103 qurtubi says the alleged consensus is disputed because al-a'mash and others disagreed; even if granted, it begs the question, while sound reports support his position — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi says the alleged consensus is disputed because al-A'mash and others disagreed; even if granted, it begs the question, while sound reports support his position. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]

- 4:43/q45/p107 qurtubi says pleasure is the criterion: wherever pleasure occurs, the ruling of required ablution follows; he contrasts this with al-shafi'i's treatment of unmarriageable kin — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi says pleasure is the criterion: wherever pleasure occurs, the ruling of required ablution follows; he contrasts this with al-Shafi'i's treatment of unmarriageable kin. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]

- 4:43/q45/p109 the report that ibn mas'ud performed ablution with nabidh is not established because its narrator abu zayd is unknown and not known as a companion of abdullah, according to ibn al-mundhir and others — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (ابن المنذر): The report that Ibn Mas'ud performed ablution with nabidh is not established because its narrator Abu Zayd is unknown and not known as a companion of Abdullah, according to Ibn al-Mundhir and others. - 4:43/q45/p110 the water whose absence permits tayammum is pure and purifying water that remains in its original qualities — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): The water whose absence permits tayammum is pure and purifying water that remains in its original qualities. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p111 al-shafi'i's other opinion requires using the water at hand and then performing tayammum for the part left without water, since the person initially possessed water — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): Al-Shafi'i's other opinion requires using the water at hand and then performing tayammum for the part left without water, since the person initially possessed water. - 4:43/q45/p112 on forgetting water in one's baggage and using tayammum, al-shafi'i's sounder view requires repeating because the water was present and the person was negligent; another view, also attributed to malik, waives repetition if it was — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (الشافعي): On forgetting water in one's baggage and using tayammum, al-Shafi'i's sounder view requires repeating because the water was present and the person was negligent; another view, also attributed to Malik, waives repetition if it was unknown. - 4:43/q45/p113 abu hanifa permits ablution with water whose qualities have changed, arguing that the indefinite “water” in the verse is linguistically general — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أبو حنيفة): Abu Hanifa permits ablution with water whose qualities have changed, arguing that the indefinite “water” in the verse is linguistically general. - 4:43/q45/p114 qurtubi answers that the generality covers kinds within the genus of water, such as rain, rivers, springs, fresh, and salt water, not other liquids such as bean or rose water — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Qurtubi answers that the generality covers kinds within the genus of water, such as rain, rivers, springs, fresh, and salt water, not other liquids such as bean or rose water. - 4:43/q45/p117 imru al-qays uses tayammam for heading toward a destination, in two poems about traveling toward a place and a spring — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (امرؤ القيس): Imru al-Qays uses tayammam for heading toward a destination, in two poems about traveling toward a place and a spring. - 4:43/q45/p118 an unnamed poet uses the verb for directing a camel toward another country when dissatisfied with the current land — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (آخر): An unnamed poet uses the verb for directing a camel toward another country when dissatisfied with the current land. - 4:43/q45/p119 al-a'sha of bahila's verse uses tayammam for setting out toward qays across difficult terrain — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (أعشى باهلة): Al-A'sha of Bahila's verse uses tayammam for setting out toward Qays across difficult terrain. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أعشى باهلة)]
- 4:43/q45/p120 hamid ibn thawr's verse uses the verb to ask where someone directed herself, illustrating its sense of intending or setting out — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (حميد بن ثور): Hamid ibn Thawr's verse uses the verb to ask where someone directed herself, illustrating its sense of intending or setting out. - 4:43/q45/p121 two cited verses use mayammam for a person who directs himself toward a noble house, illustrating the word's sense of aiming or being directed — QURTUBI-FULL, d. 671 (author): Two cited verses use mayammam for a person who directs himself toward a noble house, illustrating the word's sense of aiming or being directed. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p198 ʿubayda al-salmānī interprets it as needing to urinate, citing the prohibition on praying while holding urine and a version about compressing one’s thighs — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عبيدة السلماني): ʿUbayda al-Salmānī interprets it as needing to urinate, citing the prohibition on praying while holding urine and a version about compressing one’s thighs. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(عبيدة السلماني)]
- 4:43/q45/p199 some early authorities, including the ḥanafī school, define legally relevant drunkenness as inability to distinguish a man from a woman — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (جماعة من السلف): Some early authorities, including the Ḥanafī school, define legally relevant drunkenness as inability to distinguish a man from a woman. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(جماعة من السلف)]
- 4:43/q45/p200 al-thawrī defines drunkenness as mental disturbance, such as garbling recitation and speaking without knowing what one says — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الثوري): Al-Thawrī defines drunkenness as mental disturbance, such as garbling recitation and speaking without knowing what one says. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الثوري)]
- 4:43/q45/p201 aḥmad, and similarly mālik, regard a person as drunk when his mind changes from its sober state — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (أحمد ومالك): Aḥmad, and similarly Mālik, regard a person as drunk when his mind changes from its sober state. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(أحمد ومالك)]
- 4:43/q45/p202 some infer that drinking was initially permitted in early islam until it produced drunkenness — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (قيل): Some infer that drinking was initially permitted in early Islam until it produced drunkenness. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(قيل)]
- 4:43/q45/p203 al-qaffāl suggests that stimulating drinks that encouraged generosity, courage, and zeal may have been allowed, but deliberate use of mind-destroying drink was not — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (القفال): Al-Qaffāl suggests that stimulating drinks that encouraged generosity, courage, and zeal may have been allowed, but deliberate use of mind-destroying drink was not. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(القفال)]
- 4:43/q45/p204 al-qaffāl adds that if someone unintentionally drank enough to lose reason, the legal burden was lifted from him — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (القفال): Al-Qaffāl adds that if someone unintentionally drank enough to lose reason, the legal burden was lifted from him. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(القفال)]
- 4:43/q45/p205 janāḥ ibn ḥubaysh transmits كسلى with either ḍamma or fatḥa, as al-zamakhsharī relates — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (جناح بن حبيش): Janāḥ ibn Ḥubaysh transmits كسلى with either ḍamma or fatḥa, as al-Zamakhsharī relates. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(جناح بن حبيش)]
- 4:43/q45/p206 the phrase means until you recover and understand what you say; it names the intended result, since an intoxicated person does not understand speech — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): The phrase means until you recover and understand what you say; it names the intended result, since an intoxicated person does not understand speech. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p207 al-zamakhsharī also permits an adjectival parse: the restriction concerns junub residents without an excuse — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الزمخشري): Al-Zamakhsharī also permits an adjectival parse: the restriction concerns junub residents without an excuse. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الزمخشري)]
- 4:43/q45/p208 to explain prayer while junub in travel, al-zamakhsharī paraphrases the phrase as not washed until washing, except when traveling — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الزمخشري): To explain prayer while junub in travel, al-Zamakhsharī paraphrases the phrase as not washed until washing, except when traveling. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الزمخشري)]
- 4:43/q45/p209 the majority prohibit junub men and menstruating women from reciting any qurʾān until washing. mālik permits a short protective verse and permits a menstruant to recite freely if she fears forgetting — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الجمهور): The majority prohibit junub men and menstruating women from reciting any Qurʾān until washing. - 4:43/q45/p210 the endpoint keeps a junub person from prayer until the whole body has been washed — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (author): The endpoint keeps a junub person from prayer until the whole body has been washed. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p211 ʿaṭāʾ and al-ḥasan reportedly require washing even if the person dies; abū ḥayyān answers with the report of ʿamr ibn al-ʿāṣ at dhāt al-salāsil, whose tayammum after fearing death was approved by the prophet — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عطاء والحسن): ʿAṭāʾ and al-Ḥasan reportedly require washing even if the person dies; Abū Ḥayyān answers with the report of ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ at Dhāt al-Salāsil, whose tayammum after fearing death was approved by the Prophet. - 4:43/q45/p212 ʿaṭāʾ, al-zuhrī, mujāhid, mālik, al-awzāʿī, al-layth, al-shāfiʿī, and muḥammad ibn jarīr do not make either act obligatory in either purification — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (عطاء والزهري ومجاهد ومالك والأوزاعي والليث والشافعي ومحمد بن جرير): ʿAṭāʾ, al-Zuhrī, Mujāhid, Mālik, al-Awzāʿī, al-Layth, al-Shāfiʿī, and Muḥammad ibn Jarīr do not make either act obligatory in either purification. - 4:43/q45/p213 the verse names washing without stating an intention requirement; abū ḥanīfa and his companions do not require intention for water purification — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (أبو حنيفة وأصحابه): The verse names washing without stating an intention requirement; Abū Ḥanīfa and his companions do not require intention for water purification. - 4:43/q45/p214 mālik's well-known position, al-shāfiʿī, aḥmad, isḥāq, and abū thawr require intention; al-walīd ibn muslim transmitted the contrary view from mālik — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (مالك والشافعي وأحمد وإسحاق وأبو ثور): Mālik's well-known position, al-Shāfiʿī, Aḥmad, Isḥāq, and Abū Thawr require intention; al-Walīd ibn Muslim transmitted the contrary view from Mālik. - 4:43/q45/p216 al-awzāʿī says contact invalidates ablution when it is by hand, but not otherwise — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الأوزاعي): Al-Awzāʿī says contact invalidates ablution when it is by hand, but not otherwise. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الأوزاعي)]
- 4:43/q45/p217 if water is too scarce to use without risking thirst, the majority permit tayammum — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الجمهور): If water is too scarce to use without risking thirst, the majority permit tayammum. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الجمهور)]
- 4:43/q45/p218 for the majority any travel allows the concession, whether or not it qualifies for shortening prayers; other jurists require a shortening journey, and another group requires travel for obedience — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (الجمهور): For the majority any travel allows the concession, whether or not it qualifies for shortening prayers; other jurists require a shortening journey, and another group requires travel for obedience. - 4:43/q45/p219 another view treats someone beyond hearing people's voices as being like a traveler — ABUHAYYAN-FULL, d. 745 (قيل): Another view treats someone beyond hearing people's voices as being like a traveler. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(قيل)]

- 4:43/q45/p247 sa'd reports that after an anṣari hosted and served wine to migrants and anṣar, they became drunk and boastful; a man struck sa'd's nose with a camel jaw, and the verse then descended before wine — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (سعد بن أبي وقاص): Sa'd reports that after an Anṣari hosted and served wine to migrants and Anṣar, they became drunk and boastful; a man struck Sa'd's nose with a camel jaw, and the verse then descended before wine was prohibited. - 4:43/q45/p248 the best definition of intoxication is being unable to know what one is saying; drunkenness muddles recitation and removes reflection and humility — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (author): The best definition of intoxication is being unable to know what one is saying; drunkenness muddles recitation and removes reflection and humility. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL]
- 4:43/q45/p249 other authorities allow menstruating and postpartum women to pass if they can avoid contamination, and otherwise forbid passage — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (بعض الأئمة): Other authorities allow menstruating and postpartum women to pass if they can avoid contamination, and otherwise forbid passage. - 4:43/q45/p251 ibn jarir prefers wine intoxication and says the command addresses someone capable of understanding responsibility, not one so drunk that he cannot understand speech, who is legally like an insane person — IBNKATHIR-FULL, d. 774 (ابن جرير): Ibn Jarir prefers wine intoxication and says the command addresses someone capable of understanding responsibility, not one so drunk that he cannot understand speech, who is legally like an insane person.
